- **Top to bottom:** "Settings" with the square back button. "Truck" beside "Driving alert". "Report for the accountant", four fields with grey example words. "Monthly reminder". "Trips · applies from the next trip". "Work schedule · trips in these hours are Business". "Trips outside the work hours". "Daily check". "Trip-start sound". "Backup, export and import". APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Settings", has each tile.
- **What MilO has and the drawing does not show** is kept, in the same style: the third number under "Trips" (how long the truck may stand still), the reminder's day, the tile of the daily check, "Use the built-in sound", and on the last tile the switch for the GPS points, the date of the last export, the safety copies, and what an export or an import came to.
- **The sentences that explain a tile are put away** behind one small grey line with an arrowhead at the end of the tile ("About the reminder"). They are the sentences the cards had. What has happened is never put away: a refused time or file, a change that was not stored, the last export.
- **The work schedule is still stored day by day:** for each of the seven days whether it is a work day, and its start and its end to the minute. The drawing shows one pair of hours for the whole week. So: while every work day has the same hours, one slider stands for the week, and moving a handle writes the hours of all seven days. When two work days differ, or when "Set each day by itself" is pressed, every work day has a slider of its own.
- **The slider** moves in quarters of an hour between 4 AM and 10 PM, and its two handles stay half an hour apart, as in the design's own script. A time can still be set to the minute: a press on the start or the end opens the time picker, as before. Hours outside 4 AM to 10 PM, which only the picker can set, are shown on a line of the whole day, midnight to midnight; a handle at its very end is 23:59. A time between two quarters stays as it is until its own handle is moved. So the slider can show every time the app can store, and with the picker every one can be set.
- **New shared components** (`core/designsystem/component/`): `SpanSlider` with `SpanScale` (the slider with two handles, and its arithmetic), `SegmentedChoice`, `ToggleButtonRow`, `QuietExpander`, `SwitchTile` and `TitledSwitchRow`, `TileButton`. **Small additions to older ones:** `StepperRow` is laid out as drawn (only Settings uses it); `ActionButton` can carry an icon and can wait; `TextEntry` can show example words; the drawing of `SwitchRow`'s switch is shared.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** The whole screen beside the drawing. Each handle dragged, to both ends of the line and as close to the other as it goes. A day switched off. Each day by itself, Friday given an earlier end, and the days made to agree again. 3:30 AM picked, which brought the whole day's line; a start picked later than the end, refused in red. "Ignore them". A file chosen as the sound, and the way back. An export. A font size of 1.3. A paired truck was drawn from sample values only: the emulator has no truck.
- **What still differs from the drawing, each on purpose.** The small grey "About…" lines. Three numbers under "Trips", the reminder's day, the daily check, the grey sentence on the sound's tile, the switch and the date on the last tile. The names of the hours under the slider stand where their hours are, so "9 AM" and "6 PM" are a few dp to the right of the drawing's even spacing. A text field's outline is lighter and the field 2 dp higher, as on every screen. The bottom bar stays on screen.
- **Tests and gate.** 55 new unit tests: `SpanScaleTest` (19), `ScheduleSliderTest` (11), `WorkWeekTest` (10), `WorkHoursFormatTest` (7), `ScheduleWeekStateTest` (6) and two in `SettingsViewModelTest`. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 2,045 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Settings", "Trip-start sound", the end of the component list under "Design system", and file names in four other entries), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-60 to NL-74; checks 15, 16 and 118 corrected), ARCHITECTURE (the sound's row in the table of system services; "card" to "tile" in five places), README (the sound in "Habits" and in "When a trip starts"; the export button's name).

**`[FINDING]` What the slider and the emulator taught**
- **A drag has to choose its handle where the finger came down.** Compose reports a drag only once the finger has moved about 8 dp, and hands over the place it has reached by then. A finger that came down at the edge of a handle's 48 dp and moved away from it was out of reach by that time, and took hold of nothing. `SpanSliderTouch.kt` now waits for the first touch itself and measures from there.
- **"A press near it counts" is not a touch target where something else can be pressed in that room.** Compose lets a press within 48 dp of a small control count as a press on it, but only where nothing else takes the press. A day's own start and end stood 4 dp above that day's slider, and the slider took the presses beside them. Those two times now take up 48 dp. The week's large hours have free room around them and are left as drawn.
- **A whole screen in one picture.** `adb shell wm size 1080x6600` makes an emulator's screen that high, and one screenshot then holds all of Settings. `adb shell wm size reset` puts it back.
- **A preview of the whole screen in four states** can be shown on a device, as Home's can (below): `SettingsPreview` in `feature/settings/SettingsScreenPreviewKt`, with `--es parameterProviderClassName com.shawnkowalchuk.milo.feature.settings.SettingsSamples --ei parameterProviderIndex N`, N from 0 to 3. State 1 has a paired truck, which an emulator cannot have.

**`[DEBT]` Two comments in the theme say a token still waits, and it no longer does**
- **What was cut:** `Shape.kt` says that no component uses `wideButton` and `segment`, and `Type.kt` that nothing uses `spanFigure`. Settings uses all three since today.
- **Why:** other screens were being laid out on a branch of their own at the same time, and both builders would have rewritten the same sentences.
- **Cost to fix later:** minutes. Correct the two sentences once the branches are together. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `SegmentedChoice.kt` and `ScheduleTile.kt`.

**`[CHANGE]` The Report screen and the edit screen are laid out as the owner drew them, and the pairing screen in the same language**
Shawn's drawings of the monthly report and of "Edit trip" are built as drawn: the same parts in the same order, with the drawings' sizes, spacing, type, colours and words. The pairing screen has no drawing and is laid out from the same parts. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. No change to the database, the settings or the dependencies, and nothing under `platform/trip/`, `platform/bluetooth/` or `core/trip/`. **Nothing of it has been on the phone:** device checks NL-75 to NL-94.
- **The Report screen, top to bottom.** "(Month) report" over "For the accountant". "Period" (a month or a date range; not in the drawing, Shawn asked for it). "Business" with the km and the trips, beside the amber "Status". The PDF's tile with a small page, the file's real name and "Preview PDF". "Sent to" with "Change". The lime "Email the report", then "Save PDF and CSV" and "Mark as sent". "Sent reports" at the end (not in the drawing), each with Remove.
- **Three buttons do something new.** "Preview PDF" makes the PDF if it has to and opens it in the phone's PDF viewer; it replaces "Create PDF" and "Open PDF". "Save PDF and CSV" hands both files to Android's share sheet in one go. "Mark as sent" records a report as sent without the email app, after a question that says what it does.
- **`[DEBT]` paid: "mark as sent".** The entry of 2026-10-06 (phase 4) logged that a report answered "Not sent" by mistake could only be sent again. "Mark as sent" is that missing way. It writes the row "I sent it" writes, and the event log says it was marked by hand.
- **One question was reworded.** The question before Remove said that a month could only be marked again by sending its report again. It now names "Mark as sent" as well.
- **"Reminding daily" is said only when it is true.** The Status tile asks the monthly reminder's own rule (`judgeReminder`), as Home's amber tile does: last month, the reminder switched on, its day passed, a Business trip in the month, no report sent.
- **The edit screen.** "When" (the date as one wide button; "Start" and "End" side by side; the switch), "Where", then "Distance, km" beside "Saved as" with "Business" over "Personal", a grey line that says who chose, "Save", and "What MilO recorded" in a tile with a dashed outline and a quiet "Restore". Every refusal, the question before leaving, and the differences between editing and adding are as they were.
- **The pairing screen.** A small label over a tile for each part: the truck, what is in the way, the phone's devices as rows. "Pair" is lime while no truck is stored; with a truck, "Pair again" and "Use this one" are quiet. Every state and every button is kept.
- **`[DECISION]` Where MilO's words differ from the drawing's, because the drawing's are not true of MilO.** The file is "Mileage-2026-09-(name).pdf", not "MilO-2026-09.pdf". The sentence ends "A CSV copy can be saved with it", not "comes with it": the email carries the PDF only, and changing what is emailed was not part of this. "Export CSV" stands beside "Preview PDF", because it is the one press that needs no name.
- **`[DECISION]` What the drawings took off the screens.** Explaining sentences that are not drawn: how the report's total is added up, the sentence under "Export CSV", the sentence under the title of a recorded trip's edit screen, the note under the two addresses, and the note in "What MilO recorded". What they said is still true and is in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA. A trip that is typed in keeps one sentence under its title.
- **New shared parts,** each a new file in `core/designsystem/component/`: `ScreenTitleWithLine`, `ValueButton`, `ChoiceButton`, `LinkButton`, `WideButton`, `PrimaryIconButton`, `ReportIcons`, `PageThumbnail`, `DashedTile`. **Five older shared files got a small addition** that leaves every other screen as it was: `FigureText` (one size), `TextEntry` (a figure in a tile), `SwitchRow` (a quiet switch in a tile), `RowButton` (it can wait), `Type.kt` (the two text styles that were waiting: 28 and 20 at weight 600).
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** Every button of the Report screen pressed: the PDF opened in Drive's viewer, the share sheet said "Sharing 2 files", a month was marked as sent and removed again, a date range was picked, and Gmail opened (it has no account there). A trip was added by hand and a recorded trip was edited on the new form. The pairing screen was drawn with no device; its other states only as previews. All three at a font size of 1.3.
- **Tests and gate.** 19 new unit tests: `ReportTilesTest` (the status, the reminder's line, the sender, the PDF's name), `ReportMarkingTest` (what marking stores and logs), one each for the new icon and the short date. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 2,009 unit tests and no lint finding. The view model's two new presses have no unit test of their own; what they store is tested through `ReportRecords`.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the Report screen, the edit form, the pairing screen, the end of the component list), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-75 to NL-94, and the names that changed in older checks), README (one paragraph).

**`[FINDING]` Three things seen while laying these screens out**
- **Android breaks a line at none of the hyphens of a file name.** "Mileage-2026-09-Sam-Driver.pdf" was cut as "…Sam-Driv" and "er.pdf". The Report screen now adds a place to break after each hyphen (a character with no width). The name itself is unchanged, and a screen reader is read the plain name.
- **The drawings can be rendered without a network.** The two canvas files were turned into plain pages (the placeholders filled in, the font taken from MilO's own `sora.ttf`) and drawn by Chrome without a window. The side-by-side pictures were made from those.
- **Gmail's welcome screen stays on top of MilO** on an emulator without an email account: Back from it puts MilO's task away, and opening MilO again shows Gmail. "Did you send it?" was asked all the same the next time the Report screen was opened.

**`[DEBT]` Three screens built side by side each wrote the same small helper**
Home, Trips, and Setup with the Log were laid out at the same time on 2026-10-07, each on a branch of its own, and put together afterwards.
- **What was cut:** two of them needed the same thing, a control that is drawn lower than 48 dp but keeps 48 dp for the finger without taking up more room than it is drawn in, and each wrote it: `takesUpOnly` in `PressArea.kt` (Trips) and `takesDrawnHeight` in `DrawnHeight.kt` (Setup and the Log). `AttentionTile` has an older copy of its own for its one button. All three do the same job.
- **Why:** the builders could not see each other's work, and merging three helpers into one while putting the branches together would have changed code that had just been looked at on an emulator.
- **Cost to fix later:** small. Keep one (`takesUpOnly`), point the other two at it, look at the Setup rows, the Trips buttons and Home's amber tile once more. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `DrawnHeight.kt`.

**Closed 2026-10-07 (the same day):** `takesUpOnly` is the one helper. The Setup rows' buttons and `AttentionTile`'s button use it, `DrawnHeight.kt` and `AttentionTile`'s own copy are deleted, and the tag is gone from the code. Read side by side, `takesDrawnHeight` did exactly what `takesUpOnly` does. `AttentionTile`'s copy differed in one case: it took 4 dp off its button however high the button was, and `takesUpOnly` leaves a button alone once it is higher than 48 dp. That happens when the button's words run onto a second line, or with a font so large that one line needs more than 48 dp; the amber tile is then 4 dp higher than before, and its button no longer sticks out of its place. On the emulator neither happened with "Open Setup", up to a font size of 2.0. Seen on an emulator (Android 16): Home's amber tile, Setup with three rows that have a button, and Trips with a trip's three buttons, each a picture of the build before against one of this build, the same to the pixel. On the phone it is device check NL-99.

**`[CHANGE]` Home plays the truck's arrival as drawn: "Connecting…", "Connected", then the trip**
Shawn asked for the truck's tile to be "animated same as the design", and sent three pictures of its "Connecting…". Yesterday's entry decided not to build that look. This entry builds it. It also gives Home's new layout the two states beside a parked truck, which the parked rule brought yesterday evening and the new layout did not have. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. Nothing under `platform/trip/`, `platform/bluetooth/` or `core/trip/` was changed, no dependency was added, and no setting or table. **Nothing of this has been on the phone, and no truck was involved:** device checks NL-31 to NL-39.
- **The three looks of the truck's tile,** to the design's sizes and times. Not connected: grey dashes, still. Connecting: lime dashes that run toward the truck, one dash and one gap every half second; a Bluetooth mark that fades to a fifth and back every 0.9 seconds, in a lime ring; and a ring that spreads from it and fades every 1.2 seconds. Connected: the solid lime line with a light along it every 1.8 seconds, and a ring from the truck every 2.4 seconds. A unit test holds each time and each size against the design's file (`TruckLinkMotionTest`).
- **When the arrival plays.** Home is open and in front, and the truck goes from not connected to connected. The tile says "Connecting…" and "Found the truck over Bluetooth" for 2.6 seconds, as in the design's own file. If the connect started a trip, which is the ordinary case, Home keeps the layout with the Start tile for those 2.6 seconds, then shows "Connected" and "Trip recording started" for 1.2 seconds, and then fades to the trip in 0.3 seconds. If it started no trip, the tile then shows the state it is in.
- **The trip never waits for it.** It is recorded from its first second, with its sound and its notification. Only what Home shows is held back.
- **When nothing is played.** Home opened beside a connected truck, or during a trip: shown at once. A trip started with Start: at once. Start pressed during the arrival: the trip at once, and still one trip. A trip that starts beside a parked truck when it moves: at once, because nothing connected. The truck going again during "Connecting…": "Not connected" at once. And what happened while MilO was in the background, or another screen was showing, is never played afterwards.
- **`[DECISION]` "Connecting…" is shown after the truck was found, not before.** Android tells MilO that the truck is connected, never that it is about to be. That has not changed. So the look is how Home passes the news on, for a fixed time; it is not a state of the truck. It is Shawn's design and his decision, and it replaces the `[DECISION]` in yesterday's entry below. Its price: for up to 3.8 seconds Home shows "Start trip" although a trip is being recorded. A press then does no harm.
- **Beside a parked truck.** Two more states of the truck's tile, both in its connected look: "Truck connected and parked", and after three days "Truck connected. MilO has stopped watching it", each with the sentence the old Home had. The titles are the notification's. The Start tile's small line says "Or it starts when the truck moves", and after three days "Press here before you drive off". Eleven states now; APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA has the table.
- **With the phone's animations switched off** nothing moves and nothing fades. The stages still follow one another at the same times, as the design's own file does when a browser asks for less motion. If Shawn would rather have Home change at once then, it is one condition in `homeShown`.
- **How it is built.** The stages are a holder of plain values (`ShownArrival`: what was seen, which stage shows, when it runs out), apart from all drawing. `homeShown` plays it over what the view model publishes; `HomeScreen` collects that while it is on screen. The moving values are read while drawing only, so a frame redraws the picture and composes nothing. The movement is composed only while the tile is in view and Home is resumed. ARCHITECTURE has the paragraph.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** The connecting look, three frames a fraction of a second apart, put beside Shawn's three pictures: the dashes had moved, the ring was at three sizes and the mark at three strengths. The whole arrival, filmed three times: "Connecting…" for 2.6 seconds each time, "Connected" for 1.2 to 1.4, and the trip fully there 4.2 seconds after the truck arrived. Frames drawn in two seconds: 100 to 120 while the connecting look was in view, none with the tile scrolled below the screen's edge, none in the look that does not move, none with MilO in the background, none with the phone's animations off. In a throwaway copy with a stand-in for the truck (below), through the real trip controller: the arrival on a link event, and on "the truck appeared" followed by the link a second later; Start pressed during "Connecting…", which showed the trip at once; MilO opened a few seconds after a connect, and Home returned to from Trips, both with the trip showing at once and nothing played; MilO opened beside a truck it had not noticed, which played the arrival; End with the truck connected; and "Truck connected and parked" ten minutes after a trip stood still, with a press of Start from there.
- **What still differs from the drawing.** The drawing shows "Connecting…" standing; MilO shows it for 2.6 seconds after a connect. The Bluetooth mark of the connecting look is drawn with the line of the idle one (2.0 on the icon's grid; the design has 2.2 there), a third of a pixel on this screen. The rest is as listed yesterday.
- **Tests and gate.** 51 new unit tests: `ShownArrivalTest` (24), `HomeShownTest` (13, with a clock of the test's own), `TruckLinkMotionTest` (8), and 6 more in `TruckStateTest` for the parked states. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,913 unit tests and no lint finding. One lint check is switched off at one function, with its reason written beside it: the first value of what Home draws is read from the view model's state directly, and the state is collected from there on.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the Home entry: step 6, the table, the gotchas; the truck's tile under "Design system"; the summary), ARCHITECTURE (where the arrival is played), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-31 to NL-39; NL-20 and NL-23 corrected; four of the parked rule's checks, whose words for Home were the old layout's), README (two sentences, and Home's words in the parked rule's paragraphs).

**`[FINDING]` Looking at the truck's tile on an emulator without throwaway code**
- **A preview can be shown on a device.** The debug build carries Compose's preview activity. `adb shell am start -n com.shawnkowalchuk.milo/androidx.compose.ui.tooling.PreviewActivity --es composable com.shawnkowalchuk.milo.feature.home.HomeScreenPreviewKt.HomeTruckArrivesPreview` plays the arrival over and over. `HomeConnectingPreview` holds "Connecting…". `HomeTruckStatePreview` draws Home in one state of the truck: add `--es parameterProviderClassName com.shawnkowalchuk.milo.feature.home.TruckStates --ei parameterProviderIndex N`, with N from 0 to 10 in the order of `TruckState`. The release build has no such activity.
- **A stand-in truck, for one run.** To see the real trip controller play it, a copy of the build read the truck from a flag in place of Bluetooth, and took "the truck connected" from an adb broadcast. Three small changes under `app/`, taken out again before the commit. It showed what no preview can: the states the real trip controller publishes, in their real order, play the arrival as the tests say, on both of the ways a truck is noticed.
- **The fade loses its first frames on the emulator.** Composing the recording layout takes the emulator about a tenth of a second, so the fade is seen for about 0.2 of its 0.3 seconds. On the phone that may differ; NL-31 asks.
- **`screenrecord` only stores frames that changed.** A film of a still screen has no frames, which makes a quick check that nothing moves.
- **Seen in passing, not changed.** When a trip ends, the line "From (address)" goes one frame before the layout changes, so the lime tile is a line lower for a moment. It looks like the order in which the view model's sources report the end of a trip. This work did not touch what decides it.
- **The first try used Compose's `Crossfade` for the change of layout, and was taken out.** It draws the leaving layout for one more frame with the new state in it. A press of Start then showed "Connected" and "Trip recording started" on the old layout for a frame. The two layouts now each have their own place, and only the hand-over fades.

**`[CHANGE]` Trips is laid out as the owner drew it, and each day opens when it is pressed**
Shawn's drawing of Trips is built as drawn: the same parts in the same order, with the drawing's sizes, spacing, type, colours and words. He added one thing to it that night: "for the trip menu can we have each day expand when clicked. so it shows the total day driven and when clicked expands and shows each individual trip". That is built too. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. No change to the database, the settings or the dependencies, and nothing under `platform/` or `core/trip/`. **Nothing of it has been on the phone:** device checks NL-40 to NL-49.
- **What Trips shows, top to bottom.** "Trips" and two square buttons, the month before and the month after (the second faint on the current month). The lime tile: "October 2026 · Business", the month's Business km in the largest figures, the number of trips, and a dark pill, "Not submitted" or "Submitted". Two small tiles: "Personal" ("22.0 km · 3") and "Add missed trip". The switch "Show deleted and discarded". Then one tile per day.
- **Each day is closed until its heading is pressed.** Closed, it shows the day ("Today · Tue, Oct 6") and what it adds up to ("4 trips · 48.2 km business"). A press shows its trips, another puts them away. Every day starts closed, today too. Which days are open is kept by the screen's ViewModel, so it outlives a turn of the phone and a visit to the edit screen or the Report screen; it is forgotten when another month is shown and when Trips is left for another screen of the bottom bar.
- **A trip's row:** where it went, under it in grey its times and what it is saved as, its km at the right. A tap brings up three buttons, "Edit", "Personal" or "Business", and "Delete".
- **Where the numbers come from.** Nothing is counted in a new way. The month's and each day's figures are `monthSummary`'s, as before, so the lime tile's km are still the report's total.
- **Where everything the old screen could do is now.** The way to the Report screen is the pill (the text button "Report for the accountant" is gone). The two month buttons are the squares beside the title. "Add a missed trip" is the tile "Add missed trip". Whether and when the month was submitted: the pill, and for a submitted month a line under the figures with the dates. "Changed since the report was sent" is a tile of its own with a button to the Report screen. Personal, and trips that are not sorted yet, are in the Personal tile. Edit, mark, delete with its question, Restore and "Count this trip" are the buttons under a trip. "Added by hand" and "edited", "ran past schedule", and why a trip is not counted are on the trip's grey line. The trip in progress has its tile above the days. "That trip could not be changed" is a tile under the switch.
- **`[DECISION]` Four things were added that neither the drawing nor the request has. Each is Shawn's to undo.**
  1. *After a save on the edit screen, the saved trip's day is open.* Until now Trips came back with the saved trip in front of Shawn. In a closed day it would be out of sight and look as if it had not been saved, which is how a trip once got stored twice (2026-10-06).
  2. *A day's heading says "· 1 not counted" while the switch lists a deleted or discarded trip in it.* A closed day would otherwise hide where they are.
  3. *A submitted month has a line under its figures* ("Submitted on Oct 6, 2026", with the day it was sent again and the revision). The pill holds two words, not a date, and the dates were shown before.
  4. *The warning for a month that changed since it was sent has a button "Report for the accountant".* The old button stood right under the warning; the pill is further up.
- **What went away.** The line "Tap a trip to edit it, mark it Business or Personal, or delete it." is not in the drawing. The asterisk after a typed trip's times became the words "· added by hand" or "· edited"; the report for the accountant keeps its asterisk.
- **What changed in the words, to match the drawing.** "Show deleted and discarded" (it ended in "trips"), "Add missed trip" (it had an "a"), "Edit", "Personal", "Business" and "Delete" on the buttons (a screen reader is still read "Edit trip", "Mark as Personal", "Delete trip"), "Mon, Oct 5" for a day (a screen reader is read the date in full), and a trip's times with "AM" or "PM" once where both share it ("5:30 – 5:41 PM"). The question before a delete and the sentence in Settings that name the switch follow, because they quote its string.
- **What still differs from the drawing.** Every day has an arrowhead and starts closed (asked for). The top line stands under the phone's own status bar, as on Home. The arrow in "Shop → Home" is thinner than drawn (Sora has none; the `[FINDING]` of 2026-10-06). On a screen wider than the drawing's 390 dp, "Add missed trip" fits on one line. A trip typed in says "· added by hand", so the test rows are longer than the drawn ones.
- **Shared parts, added to the design system:** `ExpandableTile`, `SteppedTitle`, `SmallTile` and `SmallLinkTile`, `PillButton`, `ActionButtonRow`, `PressArea` with `takesUpOnly`, `ChevronIcons`, and `placesLine` for where a trip went. **Small additions to parts that were there:** `FigureText` (a size for the month's total), `SwitchRow` (`quiet`), `SquareIconButton` (the colour of a greyed icon), two text styles in `Type.kt`, and two sentences of comment in `Color.kt`. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Design system", has what each is for.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** Seven trips typed in on three days through "Add missed trip"; every day closed; a day open; a trip's buttons; marking Personal and back; a delete, the deleted trip listed, Restore; a trip started with Home's button ("In progress") and ended (a discarded trip with "Count this trip"); an empty month; the pill's way to the Report screen; a month recorded as submitted and then changed (the emulator's mail app was opened and nothing was sent; "I sent it" was answered to see the look); a turn of the phone; the edit screen and back; a font size of 1.3; a day opened with animations off. Put beside the drawing at the same scale, the lime tile, the pair of small tiles, the switch, a day's rows and the three buttons are as high and as far apart as drawn, to within a dp or two.
- **Tests and gate.** 39 new unit tests: `OpenDaysTest` (7), `TripsWordsTest` (18), `TimeSpanFormatTest` (7), `TripPlaceLineTest` (5), `ChevronIconsTest` (2). `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,744 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the Trips part of "Trip log", and the new parts at the end of the "Design system" list), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-40 to NL-49), README (three lines), ARCHITECTURE (one line of a diagram).

**`[FINDING]` What laying out Trips showed, for the screens that follow**
- **A single line of text is never lower than its typeface asks for.** The theme sets the three largest figures on a line as high as the figure. Compose does not go by that for one line: the 44 of the month's total was 55 dp high, and the lime tile 12 dp higher than drawn. `FigureText` now tells that one size to take up the figure's own height. **Home's 56, the km of a trip being recorded, is still about 14 dp higher than drawn.** It was left alone: it is Home's, and this work was Trips.
- **A part that reaches past its own box must answer "how high would you like to be?"** The buttons under a trip are made equally high by asking each one that first. The part that gives a button its 48 dp for a finger did not expect the question, and MilO closed the moment a trip was tapped. Seen on the emulator and put right before anything was committed. A crash of a screen takes the trip service down with it, so a new shared part is worth tapping through once.
- **A control drawn lower than 48 dp can keep 48 dp for the finger without growing.** The pill (30), the trip's buttons (44) and the switch (28) are drawn as drawn; what is pressed is laid over them and reaches into the free room around. The emulator's own list of what is on screen shows each as 48 dp high. `AttentionTile` does the same in its own way and could use `PressArea` one day.
- **With animations off, a list still needs two frames before it knows how high an opened tile is.** Without the wait, a day opened at the lower edge stayed below it.
- **Android's code check wants a plural for any count followed by words,** also where one and several read alike ("1 not counted").
- **Home and Trips now write two things differently, until Home is given the same parts:** a trip's times ("12:40 PM – 1:07 PM" on Home, "12:40 – 1:07 PM" on Trips, `formatTimeSpan`), and the words for a missing address (grey on Trips, `placesLine`).
- **Still open from the first package:** what scrolls ends in a straight line above the floating bar. A day opened low on the screen shows it.

**`[CHANGE]` Setup and the Log are laid out as the owner drew them, and the app's icon is the app's mark**
The second and third of the bottom bar's screens to get the design's own layout, after Home. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. Nothing a row or a button does has changed, with two exceptions that are said below: the Log has five chips where it had fourteen, and sharing the log asks first. Nothing of this has been on the phone: device checks NL-50 to NL-59.
- **What Setup shows,** top to bottom as on the drawing: "Setup"; a tile with the number of rows that are ready, "of 14 ready" beside it, "2 to fix · 1 to confirm" at the right, and a bar; the label "Android" and one tile with the ten rows; the label "This Xiaomi phone (HyperOS)" and one tile with the four, on a Xiaomi phone only. A row is a dot, its name, its sentence and its button.
- **Where the numbers come from.** `setupSummary` counts the checklist's own rows, each once, by its state: ready (in order, or confirmed by Shawn), to fix, to confirm. The four counts add up to the number of rows, and a unit test holds them to that.
- **`[DECISION]` A row MilO could not read is counted by itself, as "1 not checked".** The drawing has three kinds of row and the checklist has four. The fourth is "MilO tried to find out and could not": the pairing check failed or has not finished, or Autostart gave no reading. It is not "to fix", because nothing says it is wrong. It is not "ready". And it is not "to confirm", because for the truck's row there is nothing Shawn could confirm. So it has a count and words of its own, shown only while there is such a row.
- **`[DECISION]` The count is of every row.** The line it replaces counted only the rows Home warns about. The drawing counts "Physical activity", which is a recommended row, so the tile counts all fourteen. Home's warning still goes by the required rows. The two can differ in one direction only: the tile can say "1 to fix" with no warning on Home.
- **The rows that are to be fixed stand first in their group,** as drawn: on the drawing the truck's row and "Physical activity" are at the top, above the eight ticks, which are in the checklist's own order. `toFixFirst` does that and nothing else: a row to confirm stays where it is, as drawn. A row that is put in order moves down to its usual place. README's "The rows, in order" and two device checks say so now.
- **A row's button stands at the end of the row, as drawn, when there is room for it** (`buttonStandsBeside`): one button, the text keeps at least half its width beside it, and the sentence needs no more than three lines there. Otherwise the buttons stand under the text, as before. **The rows kept their full sentences.** The drawing writes "Needed for the driving alert" where the app says three sentences, so on the phone several rows that are drawn with a button beside them will have it under their text. At the phone's width (392 dp, tried on an emulator) the truck's row has "Pair truck" at its end; a battery row with "Open settings" has it under the text.
- **What the Log shows:** "Log" with the square share button; the chips "All", "Trips", "Bluetooth", "Android Auto", "Errors"; then the lines, each with its time, a small coloured tag and its words, a hairline between two lines.
- **`[DECISION]` Thirteen categories under four chips, and five of them under "All" only.** Trips: `TRIP`, `GRACE`, `ADDRESS`. Bluetooth: `TRIGGER`, `PAIRING`. Android Auto: `ANDROID_AUTO`. Errors: `ERROR`, `CRASH`. Under "All" only: `PROCESS`, `SERVICE`, `LOCATION`, `DRIVING`, `REPORT`. **This is less than the screen could do yesterday,** when every category had a chip: the list can no longer be narrowed to `PROCESS` or `LOCATION` alone. The drawing has five chips, and those five kinds are not a trip, the truck's connection, Android Auto or a failure. Three older device checks named a chip that is gone (`PROCESS`, `REPORT`); they now say where those lines are found. If it is missed on the road, a sixth chip "Other" is one constant in `LogGroup`.
- **`[DECISION]` A trigger line is tagged "BT".** The drawing's tags are TRIP, BT, AUTO, SERVICE and ERROR. Four are the log's own categories or a short form of one. "BT" is none; the lines the drawing gives it ("Truck connected.") are the log's trigger lines. Every trigger line is a look at the truck's Bluetooth connection, and says what it found, so it carries "BT". The price: a trigger caused by the Start button or by a timer is tagged "BT" too, and the shared text file still says `TRIGGER`. Every other category keeps its stored name on its tag.
- **`[DECISION]` The date is a label above each day's tile, and today has none.** Each line used to carry its full date and time. The drawing shows the time alone. So the lines of one day are one tile, the day stands above it ("Tue 2026-10-06"), and it stays at the top of the list while that day's lines are scrolled through. Today's tile has no label, as drawn. **The seconds were kept,** though the drawing shows minutes: two lines a few seconds apart are what the log is read for. The time's column is wider than drawn for it.
- **`[DECISION]` Sharing the log asks first.** The drawing has a square button and no room for the sentence that said, before the press, what the file gives away. That sentence must come before the file is made (ENGINEERING_STANDARDS, section 12), so the button now brings up a question with the same sentence and the buttons "Share" and "Cancel". One press more than before.
- **The icon.** A lime background and the dark bold "M" of the mark at the top of Home. The letter is not drawn by hand: its sixteen points were read from the font file with a few lines of Python, and moved to weight 700 by the font's own weight axis (Sora is one file for all weights; its M is made of straight lines only). It is as large on the icon as on the mark. Measured on an emulator beside the mark: the letter takes up 34 percent of the mark's width and 33 to 34 percent of the icon's, and its upright strokes are 18 to 19 percent of its width in both. The launch screen shows the same icon for a moment. The notification icon was not changed.
- **What else differs from the drawings, each on purpose.** The second group's label is the app's "This Xiaomi phone (HyperOS)", not "HyperOS · POCO X5 Pro": MilO does not read the phone's model, and a model written into the app would be wrong on any other phone. The sentence that explains the HyperOS rows stands under their tile. The Log's time and tag are in the phone's own monospace, as decided with the typeface. The Log's lines are MilO's real sentences, two to four lines where the drawing has one or two.
- **Kept:** every row of the checklist with its sentence, its buttons and what they do; the re-reading when the screen comes to the front; the Log's 200 lines at a time and "Show older entries"; a line's details on a press; a sentence for an empty list, now one for each chip; the share's file, its "Writing the file…" and its two failures; the trimming, which the screen never touched.
- **Shared parts.** New: `TileRow` (a row that draws its slice of a tile, so that a long list in one tile can be lazy), `GroupLabel`, `Tag`, `takesDrawnHeight`. Changed, each by a small addition: `StatusRow` (it can be told to put one button at the end of the row; unless told, it does what it did), `ProgressLine` (a thick form), `FigureText` (a fourth size), `ScreenTitle` (its action can wait, and lines up with the tiles), `MiloIcons` (Share), `ChipChoice` (draws its own chips at the design's size), `AppHeader` (one line: it passes on whether its button can wait), and in the theme one text style (`countWords`) and the line of `displaySmall`. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA has them at the end of the component list.
- **Not touched:** the checklist's rules and what it reads from the phone (`platform/system/`), the trip engine, the settings, the dependencies. In `data/eventlog/` one query changed: the log can be read for several categories at once.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** Setup with six and with eight of ten rows ready, the second being the drawing's own state (the truck and "Physical activity" to fix); both screens at the emulator's width (411 dp), at the phone's (392 dp) and at a font size of 1.3; the Log with real lines and with sample lines of two earlier days put into its storage by hand; each chip, a chip with no line, a line opened, the day labels, "Show older entries", the share's question and Android's share sheet; the icon in the app list, on "App info" and on the launch screen. Put beside the drawings at the same scale, the Log's title, button and chips stand where they are drawn, and Setup's top tile is as high as drawn (92 dp). **An emulator is not a Xiaomi phone,** so the HyperOS group, with its question mark and its empty rings, was drawn in a throwaway copy of the build that was not committed; so were "Writing the file…" and "The log could not be written".
- **Tests and gate.** 38 new unit tests: `SetupSummaryTest` (11), `LogGroupsTest` (6), `LogListTest` (8), `StatusRowLayoutTest` (7), `TileRowsTest` (4) and two in `EventLogRepositoryTest`. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,743 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Permission checklist", the Log's part of "Diagnostics and reminders", the end of the component list under "Design system"), ARCHITECTURE (one sentence about reading the log), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-50 to NL-59, and the older checks and notes that named Setup's old words, the Log's old title, or a chip by a category's name), README (the sentences about Setup's top and its first label, and about the Log).

**`[FINDING]` What laying out these two screens showed**
- **A line height of 1 does not make one line of text as high as itself.** `Type.kt` gives the three largest figures a line as high as the figure. Compose still keeps the typeface's own room above the first line and under the last, so a figure of one line stands in a box 1.26 times its size. Setup's 36 sp count was 45 dp high and its tile 9 dp higher than drawn. It takes the mode "Tight" and the trimming of both ends together; "Tight" alone changes nothing. `displaySmall` has both now. **Home's 56 sp figure of a trip being recorded does not:** on an emulator its box is 71 dp high, 15 more than its line height says, so that tile is probably higher than drawn by as much; it was not put beside its drawing for this. It was left alone, because Home was laid out and looked at before this was known. One line in `Type.kt` would change it.
- **Material's chip cannot be the drawn chip.** It keeps 16 dp beside its words and 48 dp of height, so two lines of chips stood 12 dp apart where 6 are drawn. `ChipChoice` now draws its own. Compose lets a press within 48 dp of a smaller control count as a press on it; a tap 4 dp above a chip chose it on the emulator. Whether a thumb in the truck agrees is device check NL-57.
- **A long list in one tile.** A tile around a lazy list cannot be lazy. Each row draws its own slice of the tile instead, the first and the last with the round corners.
- **The emulator's launcher has one icon shape,** a circle, and no setting for another. The icon was drawn under two more shapes outside the launcher, from the drawables' own numbers.

**`[DEBT]` Left open by this change**
- **`StatusRow` has two layouts.** Setup asks for the button at the end of the row. Every other screen's rows still have their buttons under the text, with the old heights. One of the two should become the only one once the other screens are laid out. Cost: one default value, and a look at every screen that has a row with a button.
- **Two copies of one small thing.** `AttentionTile` has its own way of letting a button take up only its drawn height; `takesDrawnHeight` is the shared one. The tile was not edited: other screens were being laid out at the same time and may use it.
- **Sentences in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA's "Design system" entry that this change made untrue and that were not edited,** because three screens were being documented at once and each kept to its own entries. The end of the component list says what is true. To correct when the three are brought together: "The foundation is everywhere; the design's layout is on Home only" and the sentence after it; "Tokens that wait" (`logText`, `logTags`, `logTime`, `logTag` and `displaySmall` no longer wait) and "16 at 500" under the sizes without a style; `ProgressLine`'s "that comes with Setup"; `StatusRow`'s "up to two buttons on a line of their own"; `ScreenTitle`'s "not for an action on the screen itself"; `ChipChoice`'s "Material 3's `FilterChip`" and "fourteen choices"; the pattern "A secondary action is a text button" where it names "Share the whole log"; the edge case that lists "five filters on the Log, and 11 of 14 ready on Setup" among things MilO does not do; and the entry's status line and its "Last updated".
- **"Show older entries" is still a text button,** like the other text buttons the first package left.

### 2026-10-06 (evening, the new look)

**`[CHANGE]` Home is laid out as the owner drew it**
Shawn put his phone beside his drawing of Home and asked: "why when building the interface with the supplied design does it not look the same?" He was right. The plain answer: the first package of the new look changed the materials only. The colours, the typeface, the corners, the shared parts and the bottom bar became the design's. Every screen kept the layout it had, a title over a column of cards. The documents of that package said so; the phone could not. This entry is Home's own layout, as drawn. Phone only: the Android Auto screen is untouched. **The other screens still have their old layout.** Nothing of this has been on the phone: device checks NL-20 to NL-30.
- **What Home shows, with no trip open,** top to bottom as on the drawing: the "M" mark, "MilO" and today's date, with the button to Settings; the lime tile "Start trip", which is one button; "Today · business" and the month side by side, the month with a thin bar and "89% business"; the truck's tile; the amber tile for last month's report, only while it has not been sent; and "Last trip", which leads to Trips. The warning "Setup needs attention" is an amber tile directly under the top line, and "MilO could not start this trip" stands there too.
- **While a trip is being recorded:** the lime tile shows "Recording", the kilometres so far, the minutes and "since (time)", "From (address)" once it is known, and the dark button "End trip". Under it a small Truck tile and "Today · business", then today's finished trips.
- **Where the numbers come from.** Today and the month are counted by the functions the Trips screen and the Android Auto screen already use (`todayTrips`, `categoryTotals`), from one reading of this month's trips. The month's figure is therefore the one on Trips and on the report. The percent is new: the Business share of all the month's counted kilometres, a pure function with tests (`businessPercent`). A month with no kilometres says so in words. The report tile is the monthly reminder's own rule (`judgeReminder`), and the gathering of what that rule goes by was taken out of `MonthlyReminder` into one pure function (`reminderMoment`), which the notification and the tile both call. The notification's behaviour did not change.
- **The truck's tile says what MilO really knows.** Nine states, each with its look and its words, in one enum and one function (`TruckState.kt`): not read yet, no truck paired, not connected, not connected while Setup needs attention, connected after End was pressed, connected with nothing recorded, and three while a trip is open. Whether End was pressed with the truck connected is read from the stored hold-off. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA has the table.
- **`[DECISION]` The drawing's "Connecting…" is not shown, and was not built.** The canvas draws a third look: moving dashes and a pulsing ring, "Found the truck over Bluetooth". Android tells MilO that the truck is connected, never that it is about to be, and a trip opens within the second. There is one moment that comes close: a start by the companion service that waits up to 15 seconds to be confirmed. The trip controller does not publish that moment, a trip is already being recorded during it, and this work was not to touch the trip engine. Showing the look at any other time would be decoration. If Shawn wants it, the trip controller has to say "a start is waiting to be confirmed" first.
- **What else differs from the drawing, each on purpose.** The live tile has no "Business" or "Personal": a trip is sorted when it closes. The month has one decimal ("214.0 km"), as on Trips and on the report. On a day with a Personal trip a small "Personal:" line stands under today's figure, as Home has always kept Personal apart. The rows of today's list say what each trip is saved as, and a trip that is not Business has its figure greyed. Real addresses are longer than "Supplier → Shop" and take two or three lines. "Or just connect the truck" is only shown while it is true.
- **What the drawing took off Home.** With no trip open, Home no longer lists each of today's trips or shows their drive time. Both are on the list that shows while a trip is recorded, and on Trips.
- **Shared parts, added to the design system for every screen:** `Tile` (plain, accent, amber; pressable), `TileLabel`, `TileColumn` and `TilePair` (the 18 dp gutter, the 10 dp gap, two tiles of one height), `FigureText`, `Pill`, `DotWord`, `ProgressLine`, `HeroActionTile`, `HeroButton`, `AttentionTile`, `AppHeader`, `TruckTile`, and six icons. `SectionCard` is a `Tile` underneath and looks as it did. The current icon of the bottom bar is lime, as on the drawing of Home; it was white.
- **Moved, not copied.** The words for where a trip went ("from → to", "Looking up the addresses…") went from the Trips screen to `core/designsystem/text/TripPlaceWords.kt`, because Home writes the same trips. Trips reads as before; its two tests got new import lines and nothing else.
- **Not touched:** the database, the settings, the dependencies, and everything under `platform/trip/`, `platform/bluetooth/` and `core/trip/`.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** Home with no trip, with trips added by hand, with the report tile for September (shown by the real rule, after a September trip was added), while a trip started with the button was fed GPS points, after End, each way out of Home, and both layouts at a font size of 1.3. Put beside the drawing at the same scale, four tiles are as high as drawn to within a dp (104, 116, 151 and 76 dp). "Last trip" is 2 dp higher than drawn (97 for 95), because of its arrow (the `[FINDING]` below). **An emulator has no truck,** so the real app drew "No truck paired" only, with the Setup warning that goes with it. The other looks of the truck's tile were drawn in a throwaway copy of the build that was handed each state, and that copy was not committed. With the connected look showing, the emulator drew 60 frames a second; with its animations switched off, none; with MilO in the background or another screen showing, none.
- **Tests and gate.** 50 new unit tests: `TruckStateTest` (17), `HomeFiguresTest` (16), `HomeUiTest` (12), `ReminderMomentTest` (3) and two in `TimeFormatTest`. `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,705 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (the Home entry rewritten; "Design system": the new parts, the lime icon, what is in use; the reminder's rule; the summary), ARCHITECTURE (how Home's tiles are worked out, two more back stacks, the folder list), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-20 to NL-30, and ten older checks whose words for Home or for the bar's current icon were no longer true), README (four sentences about Home).

**`[FINDING]` What the next screens need to know, from laying out the first one**
- **A tile's label is `tileLabel`, not `bodyMedium`.** Both are 12 sp. `bodyMedium` keeps the taller line of a sentence, and every label set in it makes its tile 3 dp higher than drawn.
- **The unit after a figure is pulled together by the figure's own length, not its own share.** The canvas is HTML, where a "km" inside a large figure inherits the figure's letter spacing as a length. `FigureText` does the same; a plain second text style does not, and the "km" then stands too far off.
- **A button drawn lower than 48 dp takes 48 dp of room.** Material keeps the difference free for the finger. In a tile that made the amber tile 4 dp too high. `AttentionTile` lets the button take only its drawn height and lets the finger's room reach into the tile's padding. The same will be wanted on Trips and on the Report screen.
- **Sora has no arrow.** The font file holds the middle dot, the dash and the three dots, but not "→". In "Supplier → Shop" the arrow is drawn by the phone's own font, thinner than the words, and that font makes the line 4 px higher than Sora's own. The drawing shows a heavier arrow, from the browser's font. Trips will show the same. It is the existing string; nothing was changed.
- **What scrolls still ends in a straight line above the floating bar** (first package, still open). Home is longer than the screen when the Setup warning and the report tile are both showing.
- **An emulator cannot show a truck.** Any look that depends on the truck has to be forced to be seen there. A throwaway copy of the build that steps through the states did it in one install; it must never be committed.
- **The emulator's storage, again.** It was still 94 percent full. Removing MilO with its data kept (`cmd package uninstall -k`) and installing a few seconds later worked every time, and the test trips stayed.

**`[CHANGE]` Sora is in: the design's typeface, its line heights, and the new look's checks renumbered NL-1 to NL-19**
MilO is now set in Sora, the typeface the "Bento" design is drawn in. This closes the `[DEBT]` entry further down, "The typeface is the phone's own, not the design's Sora". Phone only. No file under `feature/` changed, no string, no dependency and no Gradle file. Nothing of it has been on the phone: device checks NL-16 to NL-19.
- **The file.** `app/src/main/res/font/sora.ttf`, 111,400 bytes: the font "Sora", version 2.000, taken unchanged from Google's fonts repository (`github.com/google/fonts`, the file `ofl/sora/Sora[wght].ttf`). Shawn approved the download on 2026-10-06. A unit test compares the file with the SHA-256 of that download (it begins `84ff7096` and ends `a316e16c`), so another file cannot take its place unnoticed. `.gitattributes` now marks `.ttf` files as binary, like the sound files, so that git never rewrites one.
- **The licence** is the SIL Open Font License 1.1, "Copyright 2019 The Sora Project Authors". It was read before the file was added. It lets the font be used and carried inside an app, free of charge. It asks that the copyright notice and the licence go with every copy, and that the font is not sold by itself. The licence text is `licenses/Sora-OFL.txt`, and README says so. The font file also names its copyright and its licence inside itself. **The app shows the licence on no screen.** MilO is installed on Shawn's own phone and handed to nobody. If it is ever given to someone else, the licence text has to go with it, for example on a screen of the app. That is a decision for that day, and Shawn's.
- **How four weights come from one file.** Sora is a variable font: one file that can be drawn at any weight from 100 to 800, where most typefaces come as one file for each weight. The weight is how bold the letters are. `Type.kt` lists that one file four times, once for each weight the design uses: 400 (regular), 500 (medium), 600 (semi-bold) and 700 (bold). Each entry says two things. Which weight it stands for, so that Compose picks it for a text style of that weight. And how heavy the file is to draw itself, which is called a variation setting. Without the second, every entry would be drawn at the file's own default, 400, and bold text would look exactly like regular text, with no build failing. In code it is `Font(resId, weight, variationSettings = FontVariation.Settings(FontVariation.weight(…)))`, which is a stable part of the pinned Compose (ui-text 1.12.1) and needs no opt-in. The monospace of the Log's time and tag stays the phone's own, as decided.
- **Line heights, the other half of the debt.** Until now they were Material's proportions. Every text style now names its line height as a share of its text size, as the design does:
  - *Most text stands at Sora's own line,* 1.26 times its size. The font file asks for 0.97 above the line the letters stand on and 0.29 below it, and a unit test reads both from the file. A row's main line of 14 sp was 20 high and is 17.6; a caption of 11 was 16 and is 13.9. Rows and tiles are a little lower for it, as drawn.
  - *A sentence that stands by itself has more air:* 1.4 at 13 sp (`sentence`), and 1.5 at 12 sp (`bodyMedium`), as the design gives its sentences. The 12 sp role is what the screens set their notes and explaining sentences in. The price: a row's second line, which is one line long, is about 3 sp higher than drawn. Its height did not change with this entry; it was 18 before.
  - *The three largest figures* (56, 44 and 36 sp) stand on a line exactly as high as themselves, as drawn and as before.
  - *Letter spacing* is unchanged from the first package and is the design's: the letters are pulled together by 0.04 of the text size at 56, 44 and 36, by 0.03 at 30 and 26, and by 0.02 at 22. Everything smaller has none.
  - The 1.26 is written out for every style and not left for Android to work out. A style without a line height takes over the one of whatever it stands in, a dialog or a button, and the same row would then be higher in one place than in another.
- **"MilO" can look like "Mi1O".** Sora draws the small "l" with a small flag at its top, so in the app's name it looks a little like the figure 1. That is the typeface Shawn chose, and his own drawings show the same. It is not a fault and was not worked around.
- **One thing no longer fitted, because Sora is a wide typeface.** With the font size at its largest (2.0 on the emulator), the calendar's list of years drew "2040" as "204", and the chosen year filled its pill from edge to edge. Material's calendar gives a year a place 72 dp wide that does not grow with the text. `DateDialog` now lets the text inside the calendar grow to one and a half times and no further; its two buttons grow as everywhere. It is one named value in `DateDialog.kt` (`CALENDAR_LARGEST_FONT_SCALE`), should Shawn want it otherwise. Nothing else was changed for width: no token and no other component. Sentences break in other places than before, and with the font size turned up "Start: choose a time" and "End: choose a time" take two or three lines each. Nothing else was cut off.
- **The new look's device checks have numbers of their own now: NL-1 to NL-19.** They were 265 to 279. Work that was done at the same time, and is merged before this, uses 265 to 285 for its own checks. So the new look's checks got a series of their own, as the driving alert (DA-) and the daily check (NR-) have. Their order is the same: 265 became NL-1, and 279 became NL-15. Every mention was changed: in DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (its status header, step 18 of its order, check 52 and the section itself), in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, and in the three entries of this log below that name them, which were edited for it. NL-16 to NL-19 are new, for the typeface: bold beside regular (NL-16), the large figures and titles whole (NL-17), long sentences (NL-18), and the largest font size (NL-19).
- **Tests.** `TypefaceTest` (7) holds what no build would notice: the file is the approved one; it has a weight axis that covers the four weights; each entry of the family asks the file for its own weight; no text style asks for a fifth weight; the line height written in `Type.kt` is the file's own; every style names its line height and letter spacing as a share of its size; and only the Log's two styles are monospace. The gate (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes with 1,655 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** With Sora, at the usual font size: Home idle and while a trip was being recorded, Trips with a trip and with its buttons open, "Delete this trip?", the edit screen, the form for a missed trip with its red lines, the calendar with its list of years and its typed date, the clock, the Report screen, Setup, the pairing screen, the Log and Settings. At a font size of 1.3: Home, Trips, Settings, the form, the calendar and the clock. At 2.0, the largest the emulator offers: Home, Setup, the Log, Settings, the form, the calendar and the clock. The letters are Sora's: they were compared with a picture of the font file made on the Mac. Bold, semi-bold, medium and regular lines can be told apart. No figure and no title was cut off, and the clock's large numbers stood whole in their boxes at all three sizes. After the fix above, every year in the calendar was whole at 2.0. What HyperOS offers as its largest font size is not known here.
- **The emulator was out of room, which is worth knowing for the next time.** Its storage was 94 percent full, and Android then refuses to install a build over the old one ("INSTALL_FAILED_INSUFFICIENT_STORAGE"). Clearing MilO's own cache there freed nothing to speak of. MilO was removed from that emulator and installed again, with its test trips copied out first and put back. The room of the removed copy only came back a few minutes after the removal, so the first tries failed all the same. This is the emulator only. On the phone MilO is never removed: that deletes the trips (STANDARDS §13).
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Design system": the typeface, the font file and its licence, the line heights, `DateDialog`, three edge cases, the status line; and the summary at the top), ARCHITECTURE (the stack table, a paragraph on where the typeface is named, and the `licenses/` folder), README (one line for developers on where the font and its licence are), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (NL-16 to NL-19, the section's opening, the status header, step 18, and two older sentences that said the phone has its own font), and the comments in `Type.kt`. `Color.kt` needed nothing: it does not mention the typeface.

**`[FIX]` Seen on the emulator: two choices in one tile read as one paragraph**
The new look was looked at on an emulator, picture by picture (Android 16). On the Report screen, in the "Period" tile, the sentence under "A whole month" ended directly above the name "A date range", with no gap at all. The two choices read as one block of text. On Settings the same kind of row had 8 dp between two choices and read correctly.
- **Why.** A choice row (`ChoiceRow`) was at least 48 dp high and had no air of its own. Once its name and its sentence were taller than that, only a gap added by the screen kept two rows apart. Settings and the edit screen add one. The Report screen does not.
- **What was done.** The row now keeps 4 dp of its own above and below its words (`spacing.extraSmall`), inside what is pressed. Two rows directly under each other are 8 dp apart whatever the screen does, and a tap in that gap chooses the row it belongs to. A row whose words fit in 48 dp is as tall as before. No signature changed and no screen was edited. The component has a preview now: two rows in a column that adds no gap.
- **What it leaves.** Settings ("Trips outside the work hours") still adds its own 8 dp, so its two choices now stand 16 dp apart, twice as far as on the Report screen. Both read as two choices; they are not yet the same. "Business" and "Personal" on the edit screen are one line each and did not move. Taking the screens' own gap out is the next package's, in `feature/settings/ScheduleCards.kt` and `feature/tripedit/TripEditCards.kt`, which this package was not to touch (APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Design system", edge cases).
- **Seen on the emulator again, never on the phone,** in the build with the fix: the "Period" tile with a whole month and with a date range chosen, a tap in the gap between the two, Settings' two choices, and the form for a missed trip.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,584 unit tests (none new) and no lint finding.

**`[FIX]` The review of the new look's foundation: two stale sentences, two things that were hard to see, and what this log had left out**
Two reviewers read the first package of the "Bento" look (the `[CHANGE]` below) before it was committed, one for the design and for readability, one for the code and the documents. Neither found a fault in what a screen does. What they found, and what was done:
- **Documents still said "cog".** The button that leads to Settings is three sliders since this look. Two sentences of APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (Home, Settings) and one line of ARCHITECTURE still called it a cog; they now say "the square button with three sliders". A few comments in the code still say it, in files this package was not to touch (`feature/home/HomeScreen.kt`, the comment above its string in `strings.xml`, two tests). They are listed in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA for the next package.
- **DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST did not say when to run the new checks.** Its status header called checks 260 to 264 "the last section", which the new section had made untrue, and said nothing of checks NL-1 to NL-15. Its list "The order on a phone that has never been set up" ended before them. The header now names both sections, and the list has a step 18: which of the new checks are done at the desk, which need a finished trip, and that NL-8 and NL-14 need the truck.
- **An empty text field could hardly be seen.** The design draws the line around a field in the colour of a control (`#2C2F36`), which is 1.2 to 1 against a tile; a mark needs 3 to 1 to be made out. MilO's fields show no sample text, so an empty one was a small grey label above a box that was barely there. Before this look the field had Material's own outline. The line is now the grey the "to confirm" ring already uses (`#656A76`: 3.0 to 1 on a tile, 3.2 to 1 on the field's own fill), still 1 dp wide. A new test in `ColorContrastTest` holds the line to 3 to 1, idle, typed in and in error, against the field's fill, a tile and the page. **This is a second place where the design's own value was not kept.** It is one value in `Color.kt` (`fieldBorder`), should Shawn prefer the drawn one after device checks NL-8 and NL-12.
- **The buttons of the calendar and the clock stood wrong.** Both dialogs are Material's, and Material lays their buttons out for its own text buttons, which are words with air around them. With MilO's filled buttons in those places, the clock's two buttons touched each other, and the calendar's sat in the dialog's round corner: 6 dp from its right edge and 8 dp from its bottom (read from the pinned Material release, 1.4.0). `TimeDialog` now keeps 8 dp between its two buttons, the gap Material itself leaves in the other two dialogs. `DateDialog` moves its buttons to 24 dp from both edges, where the other two dialogs have theirs. No signature changed.
- **A stepper's greyed-out button was too faint.** At the end of its range its sign was drawn in the design's grey for an idle icon (`#5A5F69`), which is 2.1 to 1 on the button's square. It is now the grey of secondary text (4.6 to 1), as on a main button that is switched off: greyed, and still to be made out. A third departure from the design's values. The token `idleIcon` stays, for the idle icons the design draws on a tile.
- **The comments described tokens by uses that do not exist yet.** The design's values were all written down in this package, also those whose part is not built: ten colours, six text styles, three corners. Some comments read as if screens used them ("from the edge of the screen to the tiles", for a step no screen uses). The comments in `Color.kt`, `Type.kt`, `Shape.kt` and `MiloSpacing.kt` now say which tokens are in use today and which wait, and APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA lists them ("Tokens that wait"). No token was removed. The `[CHANGE]` entry below was corrected in the same places.
- **Three places listed the colours written in XML, and no two lists were the same.** `colors.xml`, the top of `Color.kt` and the `[CHANGE]` entry below now agree with APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA: one colour in a values file (the window's background), and besides it only the launcher icon's and the notification icon's drawables.
- **What the next package needs was only in the hand-over text,** which is not in the repository. It is now written down: in the `[CHANGE]` entry below, how the text roles were given out and what that costs, the sizes of the design that have no text style, and the full list of where the design was not followed; and in the `[FINDING]` below, what the canvas draws that MilO does not do.
- **Small things in the code.** `TimeDialog` remembers its copy of the colour scheme and no longer makes a new one each time it is drawn. The status dot names all four states with no "else", so a fifth cannot be forgotten. The black behind a sheet is a named value in `Color.kt` like every other colour. Two sizes got the one-line comment the others have. Two sentences in `Type.kt` that contradicted themselves were put right. `Shape.kt` now says that three of Material's own components (its card, a bare dialog, its outlined button) do not land on the design through the theme; none of them is used.
- **One finding was not followed as written.** A reviewer read the design's units ("km" after a figure) as 13 and 14 at weight 400, and asked for a text style for them. In the canvas a unit stands inside its figure's own element and so has the figure's weight: the units are 18, 16, 14 and 13 at weight 600, and each of those has a style already. No style was added; `Type.kt` now says which is which.
- **Looked at and left as it is, and written down for the next package** (APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Design system", edge cases): the screen that is showing is marked by the lightness of its icon alone (2.6 to 1 against the others, as drawn; device check NL-10); what scrolls ends in a straight line above the floating bar; a text button that is switched off is very faint, which the theme cannot change; and the line heights are not measured from the design (the `[DEBT]` below).
- **Seen on the emulator again (Android 16), never on the phone,** in the build with these fixes: the form for a missed trip, with a grey line around each empty field; the calendar and the clock, each with its two buttons apart and clear of the corner; and the greyed-out minus beside "Day 1" on Settings. Device checks NL-10, NL-12 and NL-13 now ask for the same on the phone.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,584 unit tests (one new) and no lint finding.

**`[FINDING]` The canvas draws things MilO does not do, and its icons may want a credit: each is Shawn's to decide**
The design canvas is a drawing of the app, and in a few places it shows more than MilO does, or something else. The look was decided as "restyled, nothing rebuilt" (the `[DECISION]` below), so none of this was built. Each needs a yes or a no from Shawn before the screens are laid out as drawn, which is the second package:
- **One range of work hours for all days.** The canvas has a button for each day of the week, and one start and one end for all of them, set by dragging along a bar. MilO stores a start and an end for each day, and each is picked on the clock dialog.
- **A truck that is "Connecting…".** The canvas draws three states of the truck on Home: not connected, connecting, connected. MilO shows no "connecting" today.
- **On the Report screen: "Mark as sent", "Preview PDF" and "Reminding daily".** MilO has none of the three as drawn. It asks "Did you send it?" when Shawn comes back from the email app, and the reminder is a switch in Settings.
- **Five filters on the Log, with everyday names** (All, Trips, Bluetooth, Android Auto, Errors). MilO has fourteen categories and shows them by their stored names.
- **"11 of 14 ready" on Setup,** with a bar that fills. MilO's Setup says in a sentence how many things are not set. It has no count of what is ready and no bar.
- **The icons.** Their outlines are taken from the canvas as drawn. Several match, or come close to, icons of two free sets, Feather and Lucide, which are published under the MIT and ISC licences. Whether MilO should carry a line that credits them, and where, is open. Nothing credits them today.

**`[DECISION]` A new look for the phone screens: the owner's "Bento" design**
On 2026-10-06 Shawn asked for a new look and supplied a design canvas: eight drawn screens (Home idle, Home while recording, Trips, the edit screen, the report, Setup, Settings, Log) in a direction called "Design 5 · Bento". It is a near-black page with rounded panels a shade lighter (tiles), one bright accent, and no shadows. What was decided before any code, and why:
- **Phone only.** The Android Auto screen is drawn by the car from Google's templates and cannot be restyled. Nothing under `platform/car/` is touched.
- **MilO is always dark.** The design has one look and no light variant, so MilO stops following the phone's light or dark setting. A light variant made up by the assistant would be a second design nobody drew. The window before the first screen is dark too, so that a launch does not flash white, and the icons of Android's own bars are light whatever the phone is set to: with the phone in light mode Android would otherwise draw them dark on a dark page.
- **The accent is the canvas's default, a lime (`#C6F432`).** The canvas offers three others; none was chosen.
- **The typeface waits.** The design is drawn in Sora. The font file is not in the repository, and nothing is downloaded without Shawn's go-ahead, which is being asked for separately. See the `[DEBT]` entry below.
- **The bottom bar shows icons and no words, as drawn.** Each screen's name stays as what a screen reader says for its icon, and each of the four buttons stays at least 48 dp, Android's smallest target for a finger.
- **Everything is restyled, nothing is rebuilt.** No capability, wording, navigation or behaviour changes with the look.
- **Two packages.** The first, below, is the foundation: the colours, type, corners, spacing and every shared component. The second is the screens' own layouts as drawn (two tiles side by side, the large accent tile on Home, the truck-connection tile, the Log's coloured tags). It is not built.

**`[CHANGE]` The foundation of the "Bento" look: tokens, every shared component, the window**
No file under `feature/`, `platform/`, `data/`, `core/trip/`, `core/schedule/` or `core/report/` changed, no string, no dependency, no Gradle file, and not the launcher icon. Every shared component kept its signature, which is why no screen had to be edited. In `app/`, one line of `MainActivity` changed, and its import. Phone only. Device checks NL-1 to NL-15.
- **Tokens** (`core/designsystem/theme/`). `Color.kt` has one dark scheme with every one of Material's colour roles set to a colour of the design, so that a stock Material component lands on the design by itself; the light scheme and the light status colours are gone. Beside it `MiloTheme.colors` names what Material has no role for: the fill of a control on a tile, the text field's fill and border, the amber tile for something that is waiting (three colours and a button), the pair for an action that takes something away, the two pairs of the filter chips and the five pairs of the Log's tags. `Type.kt` is a full type scale at the design's sizes and weights, in sp. `Shape.kt` names the design's corners. `MiloSpacing.kt` keeps the five existing steps and adds six named ones the design needs (2, 6, 10, 12, 14 and 18). `MiloTheme` no longer takes or reads a light or dark setting.
- **Not every token has a user yet** (added by the review, above). The design's values were all written down, also those whose part comes with the screens' own layouts. No component and no screen uses these today: the colours `quietFill`, `idleOutline`, `idleIcon`, `logText`, `accentPressed`, the amber tile's three, `danger` and the Log's five tag pairs; the text styles `appName`, `rowFigure`, `sideFigure`, `spanFigure`, `logTime` and `logTag`, and of Material's roles the three largest figures, `headlineLarge` and `labelSmall`; the corners `wideButton`, `segment` and `appMark`; and of the spacing the two widest steps, which are older than the design. **The screens are not spaced as drawn either:** they still stand 16 dp from the screen's edge with 8 or 16 between two tiles, where the design has 18 and 10. Those two steps exist as tokens.
- **How Material's roles were given out,** in short: the accent is `primary`, and also `primaryContainer`, because in this design whatever is chosen is the accent itself and not a paler one. A bare surface is the page; the three middle "container" roles are all the tile, because Material uses them for bars, menus, sheets and dialogs, and here each of those is a tile; the highest is a control on a tile, the lowest the well of a text field. The quiet button fill is `secondaryContainer`, so Material's own tonal button and the track of its progress bar land on it. `secondary` is the light fill with dark words of the chosen chip. The amber tile is `tertiary`. The design's red is `error`. `onSurfaceVariant` is secondary text. `outlineVariant` is the divider line. Where the design has no colour for a role, the role got the nearest colour the design has, never a new one.
- **How the text roles were given out, and what it costs** (added by the review, above). The screens set a row's main line in `bodyLarge`, its second line in `bodyMedium`, and a figure in `headlineSmall`. So those three roles carry the design's row title (14 sp at weight 600; the weight is how bold the letters are, from 400 for regular to 700 for bold), its second line (12 at 400) and its tile figure (26 at 600), and the rows land on the design without a screen being edited. **The cost: everything else the screens set in `bodyLarge` is semi-bold too,** a plain note such as "Reading…" included, until the screens themselves are restyled. `labelLarge` (13 at 600) is what Material gives its own buttons, so the 24 text buttons are set in it. **Seven sizes of the design have no text style,** because nothing that is built is set in them: 28 at weight 600 (the Report screen's figure), 20 at 600 (the distance typed on the edit screen), 16 at 500 ("of 14 ready"), 15 at 700 (the letter in the app's mark), 14 at 500 (an address that is still being looked up), 13 at 400 (a bare label beside a switch) and 12 at 500 (the small lines on the accent tile).
