- **Components** (`core/designsystem/component/`), each to its equivalent in the design: `SectionCard` is a flat tile with its title as a small grey label; `PrimaryButton` is the accent with dark words; `MiloNavigationBar` is the floating bar of four icons; `ScreenTitle` is large on the four bar screens and smaller behind a square back button elsewhere, with its action as a square button; `SwitchRow` draws the design's switch itself, because Material's has a fixed size that is not the design's; `StepperRow` has two 44 dp squares; `ChipChoice` has the design's pills; `StatusRow` has a 28 dp dot with a tick, an exclamation mark, a question mark or nothing in an empty ring, and small filled buttons; `TextEntry` is a dark well with its label above it; `FigureRow`'s figure is a step larger; the three dialogs are tiles with two small buttons. `MiloIcons` are the design's line icons. Two small parts are new and for the design system's own use: `SquareIconButton` and `RowButton`.
- **Everything a screen reader and a finger had, they still have:** rows read as one stop, headings, the switch and the radio button as such, the stepper's value said after a press, whole rows that toggle, and 48 dp for every target, also where the drawn shape is 44 or 40. The text field's label is now part of the field, so it is read with it and a tap on it puts the cursor in the field.
- **Where the design was not followed, and why.**
  - *The grey of the "to confirm" ring.* The design draws it in `#5A5F69`, which is 2.5 to 1 against a tile. A mark that carries meaning needs 3 to 1, so it is `#656A76`, the same grey made just light enough (3.0 to 1). Every other pair of words and fill in the design passes 4.5 to 1 as drawn.
  - *A status row's buttons stay on a line of their own* under the text, where the design puts one button beside the text. MilO's rows have instructions a sentence or two long and sometimes two buttons; beside them the text would be squeezed. The Setup screen's layout belongs to the next package.
  - *The choice between two named things stays a radio choice* (`ChoiceRow`) with its explaining sentence, where the design draws two segments. That too is the next package.
  - *The typeface* (the `[DEBT]` below).
  - *Two marks are a little heavier than the tick:* the exclamation mark and the question mark looked thin on the dot at the tick's thickness.
  - The rest of this list was added by the review, above. None of it is a fault; it is where a shared component, kept as it was so that no screen had to change, is not what one drawn tile shows.
  - *A tile's title is always the small grey label* (`SectionCard`). Some drawn tiles have a larger white title instead (15 sp or 14 sp at weight 600).
  - *The label of a switch row and of a stepper row is 14 sp at weight 600.* The design has that for a row with a second line, and a lighter 13 sp for a bare label.
  - *A stepper keeps its two lines,* its name above and the buttons under it, and its value's place is five times the text size wide. The design has one line and 72 dp. The plus and the minus are drawn as line icons, not typed as signs.
  - *Every square button's icon is 20 dp.* The design has 18 for most and 20 for the sliders.
  - *The chips keep Material's own room beside their words* (16 dp; drawn 14), and two lines of chips stand 12 dp apart, not 6, because each chip keeps 48 dp for the finger around its 36.
  - *Buttons drawn 44 or 40 dp take up 48,* for the same reason, so there are 2 to 4 dp more air around them than drawn.
  - *The bottom bar's distance from the screen's edge* is the larger of the drawn 20 dp and what Android's own bar needs.
  - *The line around a text field and the sign on a greyed-out square button* are lighter greys than drawn, since the review (above).
- **What the design does not draw, and what was done:** a state for "MilO could not find out" (a grey dot with a question mark); a dialog (a tile with two small buttons of the same quiet colour: the dialog cannot know whether its action takes something away, and an accent on "Delete" would invite the press the question is there to slow down); a switched-off main button (a quiet grey that can still be read); an error on a text field (the design's red around it and under it).
- **The window and Android's bars.** `res/values/themes.xml` now has the platform's dark theme as its parent and paints the window, and Android's splash screen, in the page's colour; `res/values-night/` is gone, because one window theme is enough. The colour stands in `res/values/colors.xml`, the one colour written in XML beside the launcher icon's and the notification icon's; `Color.kt` says so at its top. `MainActivity` hands `MiloSystemBarStyle` to `enableEdgeToEdge`, which makes the icons of both of Android's bars light, always.
- **Tests:** `ColorContrastTest` (6) holds every pair of words and fill, and every mark that carries meaning, to the contrast above, and `WindowBackgroundTest` (1) fails if the colour in XML is not the theme's page colour. `MiloIconsTest` builds the new icons. The gate (`spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`) passes with 1,583 unit tests and no lint finding.
- **Seen on an emulator (Android 16), never on the phone.** Home, Trips, Setup, Log, Settings, the form for a missed trip with its red lines, the calendar, the clock and "Leave without saving?" were drawn in the new look. A cold start showed Android's splash in the page's colour and no white frame, with the emulator set to light and to dark, and the clock and battery icons were white in both. The bottom bar stood above the gesture line, above the three buttons when the emulator was switched to them, and was whole with the emulator on its side. At a font size of 1.5 nothing was cut off on the parts of Home, Setup, Log and Settings that were looked at. **Two states of a status row were on no screen there,** the question mark and the empty ring (the emulator has no HyperOS rows); they were drawn from the same numbers outside the app.
- **Material's clock dialog is not a tile by itself.** With the theme alone, the calendar and the question dialog came out in the tile's colour and the clock dialog in the page's colour, a little raised. Handing the clock dialog a colour changed nothing in the pinned Material release (1.4.0): it draws itself on the scheme's "surface" whatever it is handed. So `TimeDialog` makes "surface" the tile for that one dialog, and the emulator then showed it as a tile. It also gives its title a style and room under it, which Material gives it neither of.
- **What the theme could not reach,** left for the next package and listed in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Design system", edge cases): the feature screens use Material's text button directly in 24 places, which the theme makes accent-coloured words where the design draws most of them as filled buttons; and the Log's lines are not yet the design's tag and monospace time, for which the tokens exist.
- **Documents:** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ("Design system" rewritten; two sentences elsewhere that said "green" and "amber"), ARCHITECTURE (the always-dark look and its three places), DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST (checks NL-1 to NL-15; a note on how to read the older checks' words for colours; checks 52, 64 and 243, which named the old icons and colours; and the four checks that said "the cog": 111, 130, 179 and 253), and README (which icon of the bar is which, now that it has no words, and the button that leads to Settings).

**`[DEBT]` The typeface is the phone's own, not the design's Sora**
- **What:** the "Bento" design is drawn in Sora, in four weights (400, 500, 600, 700). MilO is set in the phone's own font, at the design's sizes, weights and letter spacing.
- **Why:** the font file is not in the repository, and the assistant does not download anything without Shawn's go-ahead, which is being asked for separately.
- **Cost to fix later:** small. With the go-ahead, the font file goes into `res/font/` and one line of `core/designsystem/theme/Type.kt` names it (`MiloFontFamily`, marked `// TODO(debt)`); every text style is built on that line. The font's licence has to be read before the file is added. Sora is a wider typeface than most phones' own, so lines will break in other places: every screen has to be looked at once after the change, with the large font size too (device check NL-9). **The line heights are to be set in the same pass.** Today they are Material's proportions (a 14 sp line is 20 high), where the design leaves most text at its typeface's own line, so a row of two lines comes out a few dp taller than drawn. A line's height belongs to its typeface, so measuring it against the canvas before Sora is in would be done twice.
- The event log's monospace is the phone's own for good, by decision: a second font file for two small labels is not worth carrying.
**Closed 2026-10-06 (later the same evening):** Shawn approved the download, the licence was read, and Sora is in `res/font/` (the `[CHANGE]` entry at the top of this date, "Sora is in"). The line heights were set in the same pass. The tag is gone from the code. Every screen was looked at on an emulator, with the font size turned up too. None of it has been on the phone (device checks NL-16 to NL-19).

### 2026-10-06 (evening)

**`[FIX]` The verification before the second day in the truck: the proven start and stop are unchanged, and one rule of the wait was changed**
Two reviewers read the finished work and a third ran it on an emulator, the night before Shawn drives with it: the parked rule on top of the daily "nothing recorded" check, with 1,784 tests. They returned thirteen findings, two called major and eleven minor. Each was looked at in the code. One was a fault that could lose a whole drive, and is fixed here. One is no fault of this work but matters tomorrow morning, and has no code. The rest are judgements about decisions, or small risks: none was changed, and each is written down below with what Shawn would see. **Nothing about how a trip starts from a connect, the companion callback, a reconcile or a button was changed, nor how a disconnect and the grace period end one.** One file of the rules was changed (`core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt`). Three other files of the app got a comment and nothing else (`TripState.kt`, `TripParking.kt`, `PickedUp.kt`). No table, no column, no stored value, no setting, no dependency. In this section, "the `[FIX]` entry" in the entries below still means the review's, at the end of the section.
- **What the verification showed about the day that was proven.** A reviewer ran every trigger the phone logged on 2026-10-06 (97, each with the reading the phone took) and every GPS fix it stored through the real trip controller. The morning trip came out as on the phone: 07:18:23 to 08:01:35, 41,491 m. So did the noon trip: 12:04:38 to 12:12:29, 4,729 m, with its two-second drop started and cancelled. The afternoon, on the phone one trip of 44,021 m until End was pressed at 18:03, became two: 14:22:53 to 14:40:08 with 5,661 m, and 16:06:20 to 17:08:07 with 38,252 m. The second ends in the minute the truck was parked at home. Together they are 108 m less than the one trip. Three trip-start sounds, as on the day, and no trip started by itself in either wait (76 and 43 minutes). The same came out with every trigger delivered twice, and with the process killed and restarted four times.
- **What ran on an emulator** (Android 16, API 36.1, started for this and shut down afterwards; the phone and two other emulators were attached to the Mac and were not touched). First the build as it is: installed and opened; Settings showed the row for the parked limit and the card "Daily check"; a trip started by hand was not split by a stop of two minutes, and was closed five minutes after it last moved (the limit at its minimum), at that fix, with nothing left running. Then a throwaway copy in which only the reading of the truck was replaced. A trip the truck "started" was closed the same way and MilO waited, with "Truck connected and parked". Android's own list showed MilO's one location request change from every 5 seconds to every 30. A crash and a kill of the process each brought the wait back, and no trip. Driving off started a trip from the parked place three times, without the sound: dated 16 to 26 seconds after the truck began to roll, and showing "Trip in progress" 46 to 56 seconds after. Two readings of "not connected" ended the wait, and a later "connect" started a trip with the sound. Also seen there, each for the first time anywhere: Start during a wait; End, then Start, then a stop, with the hold-off given up for the wait; a warm-up and straight off; a truck that could not be read at a restart; and the proven stop, a disconnect and its grace period, in the new build. No `ERROR` line in the whole run. **This run was made before the change below.**
- **Fixed: a drive during which usable fixes come more than two minutes apart was not recorded at all** (major; `core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt`; ADR-002, amendment 30). The review of this evening had the watch drop a first fix that nothing bore out within two minutes, so that one stray fix could not date a trip back by hours. As built, the fix after it was then a first sighting again, and was dropped in its turn. A truck that drove off while usable fixes came more than two minutes apart was never seen to move: no trip for the whole drive, Home still saying "Truck connected and parked", and no driving alert, because the truck reads as connected. The reviewer ran the phone's own afternoon with the watch's fixes thinned: at one fix every 125, 150 or 300 seconds the 38 km drive home was not recorded. **Now** the dropped fix still cannot date a trip, but it is remembered as a sighting (`ParkedWatch.seenAwayBefore`). The next usable fix that also shows the truck away from the parked place starts the trip at once, dated at that later fix, with the parked place as its first point. A usable fix that shows the truck at the parked place takes the sighting back.
  - **Run again on the phone's afternoon, with the change.** With a fix every 30 seconds the same two trips come out, to the metre, and the old and the new watch agree at every fix. Thinned to one fix every 125, 150 and 300 seconds, the drive home is recorded from 16:10, 16:09 and 16:11, with 37,876, 37,876 and 37,868 m.
  - **The price.** After a gap in usable fixes, one stray fix can start a trip while the truck stands, where it took two close together before. That trip is closed ten minutes later and discarded as too short, and MilO waits again.
  - **Why it was changed although the reviewer left it to Shawn.** The two minutes were the review's own judgement, made to keep a trip from being dated back, not to keep one from starting. Shawn's decision is that a trip starts when the truck moves again, and a missed drive is the one thing MilO must not do. It is his to reverse.
  - **Not known:** whether this phone ever delivers the watch's fixes that far apart. On 2026-10-06 its fixes during trips came 5 to 16 seconds apart. **This change has run in unit tests only, on no emulator and not on the phone.** In the log, a trip started this way says "(2 points carried over)"; device check 286.
- **No code, and it matters on the morning of 2026-10-07: the hold-off that is set on the phone tonight** (major; no fault of this work, and the build that is on the phone behaves the same). Shawn ended trip 5 by hand at 18:03 with the truck connected, so automatic start is held off, and at 18:08 the truck was still connected. If the truck's Bluetooth drops during the night, the morning's connect starts a trip as it did on 2026-10-06. If it does not drop, nothing starts the first drive: no wait exists (a wait begins only when the parked rule closes a trip), no service runs, nothing reads the truck while MilO is idle, and the driving alert is quiet because the truck reads as connected. From about 06:04, twelve hours after the press, anything that reads the truck releases the hold-off and starts a trip, and opening MilO is such a reading. **So: open MilO before driving off, and if it then says "No trip in progress", press Start trip.** From that trip on everything is automatic again: a disconnect ends it and releases the hold-off, and if the truck stays connected, the parked rule closes the trip, gives the hold-off up and waits. A reviewer showed it in a test: nothing starts at a restart three hours after End, nothing when MilO is opened after 11 hours, and a trip when it is opened after 13. Device check 289.
- **Tests for a gap a reviewer found** (minor). Every test of the proven rules ran with the parked rule switched off, which the app never does. Two things were added. `TripStateMachineProvenDayTest` gives the day's sequences to the rules with and without the parked rule, and the two must agree at every step: the connect by both paths and reported again, the morning drive to its disconnect and grace period, the two-second drop, a reconnect after the grace period ran out, End with the truck connected, a companion start that nothing confirms, a restart in the middle of a drive, and nine minutes at a standstill. `TripStateMachineParkedRandomTest` now checks, with the parked rule on, everything the random test without it checks (the stored columns of the trip, the grace period, "connected means seen", nothing left to do at a restart after a reading), and that the same event twice changes nothing: 240,000 random steps. A fault planted to try them, a grace period that a reconnect does not cancel when the parked rule is on, fails both. Not done: the older tests still stand their trucks still for hours and so still run with the rule off (`[DEBT]` below).
- **A test for where the two features of this evening meet** (`NothingRecordedWithControllerTest`). During a wait on a work day with no trip, "No trip recorded today" comes, and the trip that the truck's movement starts takes it away. The entry "The parked rule and the daily "nothing recorded" check put together", further down, says no test had the two together; one has now.
- **Looked at and left as it is,** each with what Shawn would see. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA's gotchas have them all.
  - **A wait found again after the truck was driven with MilO's process dead** (two reviewers, minor). The stored wait carries on from the old place, the first two fixes show the truck far from it, and a trip starts there. If the truck then stands, Shawn sees a finished trip with the straight line between the two places, its start and end in the same minute, dated when MilO came back. The kilometres were driven, but hours earlier. Left: whether he wants that trip is his to say. Device check 287.
  - **The watch measures from the trip's end, not from where the truck came to rest** (minor). The truck often rolls on a few metres after its last counted fix. If it rests close to the movement threshold from that fix, GPS scatter can start a trip: "Trip in progress" with no sound while the truck stands, discarded ten minutes later. No kilometre is wrong. In the real fixes of 2026-10-06 the standing truck was at most 51 m from the trip's end, against a threshold of 61 m, and nothing started. Left: the cure changes where the watch starts and what the next trip is given, which is too much for the night before a drive.
  - **A reading that lags behind a disconnect can start a trip as a wait ends** (minor). Shawn would hear the chirp when the truck finally disconnects, and see a trip discarded two minutes later. Looked for in the phone's own log: at both disconnects of 2026-10-06 (08:01 and 12:12) every reading that a profile prompted said "not connected", so the order that causes it was not seen on this phone. Left: the cure is a change of a rule. Device check 271 looks for it.
  - **One drive becomes two when the parked limit passes without counted movement while the truck is driving** (minor): MilO's process dead for more than ten minutes of a drive, fixes too poor to use for that long, or the clock set forward. The kilometres still add up. The second trip is sorted by its own start, so a drive home that is cut after the day's end time is saved as Personal. This follows from "time nobody watched counts as standing", decided with the rule. One thing was wrong and is corrected: the comment on `RESTART_GAP_LIMIT_MS` still said that a gap of up to 30 minutes is one drive. Device check 290.
  - **One fix that is wrong by kilometres, as the first usable fix of a wait, can become a finished trip** (minor). It needs a fix more than about 4.5 km off that claims an accuracy of 25 m or better. It is the distance calculation's known limit, which the watch inherits. Left.
  - **A trip that the parked rule discards counts for the daily check** (minor). On a day with such a trip the check stays silent even if the real drive is missed later. The check's own rule, older than the parked rule. Left for Shawn.
  - **After a restore by Android's backup, the stored wait can be picked up before it is taken out** (minor). The clean-up and the trip controller's first look at storage are not ordered. Home would show that a start failed, until the next trip or restart. Left.
  - **There is no End button during a wait** (minor, from the emulator). The one button reads "Start trip" then. The documents said what End does during a wait and not that it cannot be pressed; they say both now.
  - **The battery** (minor; facts, nothing measured). During a wait: one location request, about 2,880 fixes a day, nothing written, no wake lock. Device checks 271 and 288.
- **Questions for Shawn,** in the order they matter.
  1. **After End trip with the truck still connected, should MilO wait beside the truck as it does after any other trip?** Today it starts nothing until the truck disconnects, twelve hours pass, or he presses Start. The other choice is to show the driving alert while held off.
  2. **Keep tonight's change to the two minutes, or go back?** Kept: a drive is not missed when GPS is poor, and now and then a short trip is started and thrown away. Back: no such trips, and a drive with very poor GPS can be missed whole.
  3. **A trip found after MilO was dead while the truck was driven:** keep it as it is, mark it in the log and on Trips, or leave it out?
  4. **A drive that a stop cuts in two late in the work day:** the second part is Personal, by its own start. Leave it to "Mark as Business", or change the schedule's rule?
  5. **Should a trip that was discarded beside a standing truck count for the daily check?**
  6. **Does he want a way to tell a waiting MilO not to record the next drive?** There is none: the button reads "Start trip".
  7. **The battery,** once check 271 has a figure. If a night costs too much, the larger lever is to watch only while the phone reports that it is moving, which is a change of design.
- **Stale sentences corrected** while the documents were open. APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA still said in four places that no truck was paired or that detection had never met the truck, counted four notification channels where there are five and two ranges of the settings where there are three, and did not name the stored wait among what a restore takes out. DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST was out of date about the truck in three places. `README.md` told Shawn what End trip does during a wait, when there is no such button then.
- **Tests:** 1,797 unit tests where there were 1,784: three in `ParkedWatchTest`, one in `TripControllerWaitingEdgesTest`, eight in `TripStateMachineProvenDayTest`, one in `NothingRecordedWithControllerTest`. Five of them are about the change and fail without it; a sixth fails if a dropped sighting is never taken back. **Tests that were changed, and why:** in `ParkedWatchTest`, "one displaced fix that nothing bears out in time is dropped, not kept for later" and "a fix that is borne out within two minutes still dates the trip" each asserted that a second fix after the two minutes starts nothing, which was the fault. Both now assert that it starts the trip, dated at itself, and still not at the old fix. `TripStateMachineParkedRandomTest` was only made stricter.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with 1,797 tests and no lint finding.

**`[DEBT]` What the verification left**
- **The hold-off never becomes a wait.** After End with the truck connected, a truck that never disconnects is watched by nothing until twelve hours have passed and something reads it. Shawn's first question above. Cost to change: a rule, an amendment to ADR-002 and their tests, half a day.
- **A wait that is resumed after the truck was driven with MilO dead starts a trip dated now,** with the straight line between the two places. Tagged in `platform/trip/TripParking.kt`. Cost: an hour or two once Shawn has said what he wants of that trip.
- **The watch measures from the trip's end, not from where the truck came to rest.** Tagged in `core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt`. The cure is to watch from the trip's newest usable fix and still give the next trip the old end as its first point: half a day with its tests.
- **A reading that says "connected" just after a disconnect event has ended a wait starts a trip, with the sound.** The cure is a rule: such a reading should need a second one, as a minute check's "not connected" already does. An amendment to ADR-002 first.
- **One fix wrong by kilometres as the first usable fix of a wait is believed.** Tagged in `core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt`. The cure belongs in the watch: when the fixes that follow are back at the parked place, take the step back.
- **After a restore, the stored wait and the controller's first look at storage are not ordered.** Tagged in `platform/trip/PickedUp.kt`. The cure: ignore a stored wait while a restore's note is still waiting.
- **The older tests of the trip rules still run with the parked rule off.** A reviewer gave `RULES` the limit: 23 of the 193 state-machine tests failed, each because its truck last moved hours before its events, none because a truck that moved recently behaved differently. The two tests added tonight cover the proven day with the rule on. Changing the fixtures so that all of them run that way is an hour or two.
- **The change to the watch has run in unit tests only.** The emulator run of this verification was made before it.

**`[CHANGE]` The parked rule and the daily "nothing recorded" check put together**
Both were built this evening, each without the other. The check was merged first. The parked rule's commit was then put on top of it, and eight files that both had changed were joined by hand: three files of the settings code and five documents. Nothing of either was dropped.
- **This evening has two sections because of that.** This one holds the first day in the truck and the parked rule. The next one, "evening, decisions", holds the check and the Car App Library. The entries of both are as they were written, except one sentence about the number of tests, in the `[FIX]` entry at the end of this section.
- **No code had to change.** In the three settings files git joined the code by itself, and only comments had to be joined by hand. The Settings screen has ten cards now, and its card "Trips" has three numbers.
- **Where the two meet: a wait is not a trip.** The check asks only whether a trip was started today. While MilO waits beside a truck that is connected and parked, no trip is open. So on a work day without a drive, "No trip recorded today" comes at the time set, beside "Truck connected and parked". A trip that the truck's movement starts takes it away, like any other trip. This is read from the code: no test has the two together, and nothing of either has run on the phone.
- **Device check NR-15 has a condition now.** It expects a trip to start when MilO is opened beside the connected truck. With a wait stored, MilO goes back to waiting and no trip starts until the truck moves. So NR-15 is to be done only while MilO is not showing "Truck connected and parked".
- **Sentences that one piece of work had made untrue in the other's text were corrected,** in `README.md`, APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, ARCHITECTURE and the comments of the code: the three trip numbers, the ten cards, the three settings an export file does not carry yet (the check's switch and time, and the parked limit), "MilO cannot know that the truck stood still", and four references to this log that no longer named one section or one entry.
- **Tests:** 1,784 unit tests: the 1,576 from before this evening, the check's 64 and the parked rule's 144. The counts in the entries below are each without the other's tests.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes with those 1,784 tests and no lint finding.

**`[FINDING]` First day in the truck: automatic start and stop work on the phone**
Shawn paired the truck ("Baby Mav", a classic Bluetooth device) at 07:18 on 2026-10-06 and drove with the build installed that morning (phase 1 plus the schedule). Read back over adb at 18:00, with every figure taken from the phone's own databases:
- **Three trips were started by the truck, with no error or crash line all day.** Trip 3, 07:18 to 08:01, 41.5 km. Trip 4, 12:04 to 12:12, 4.7 km. Trip 5, 14:22 to 18:03, 44.0 km.
- **Both automatic paths fire.** For trips 4 and 5 the companion service's "device appeared" and the manifest receiver's ACL connect arrived in the same second; the companion path asked for the trip service first. The trip-start sound line was written each time.
- **Disconnects are seen by all three detectors.** At 08:01:37 the companion service's "device disappeared", the manifest receiver and the trip service's own receiver each reported the truck gone within a second. The grace timer ran its two minutes and the trip was closed at the disconnect, not at the timer.
- **GPS is clean.** Trip 3 stored 516 fixes and used 496, with none too inaccurate and no jumps; the biggest gap between fixes was 7 seconds. Trip 5 stored 2,258 and used 2,162, with 96 too inaccurate. First fixes arrived within about 1.5 seconds.
- **Addresses were found on HyperOS** for all three trips, within seconds of each trip closing, and the start address of a trip in progress within two seconds.
- **HyperOS did not kill MilO.** One process ran from the 06:40 install until the next install at 18:05. So waking a fully closed MilO from a Bluetooth connect is still unproven: it never had to.
- **The second in-place database conversion on the phone, version 3 to 5, went cleanly** at 18:05: all five trips' earlier columns, all 273 earlier log lines and all 2,930 GPS points were identical afterwards. A copy of the phone's data from before it is in `~/MilO-backups/` on the Mac.
- **Updating with the truck connected did not start a trip,** because Shawn had just ended trip 5 by hand and the hold-off was set.

**`[FINDING]` The truck stays connected long after it is switched off, so "Bluetooth only" does not end a trip at home**
Trip 5 did not end by itself. The phone last moved at 17:08; at 18:03 Shawn ended the trip by hand, and at 18:08 the truck was still connected. This is the phone's own Bluetooth state, not MilO's reading of it: Android's Bluetooth service listed the truck as the active hands-free device with connection state "connected", and its audio profile as connected since 14:28. Shawn says the truck had been switched off for a while and does not believe a switched-off vehicle can stay connected; the phone says it is. Either way the consequence is the same: on this truck a Bluetooth disconnect cannot be relied on to end a trip when the phone stays near the parked truck. Earlier the same day the truck did disconnect promptly, at 08:01 and at 12:12, each time when Shawn left it at work.
What it cost on the day: trip 5 ran from 14:22 to 18:03 as one trip covering the whole afternoon, with GPS recording every five seconds for the 55 minutes after it parked.

**`[DECISION]` A trip ends when the truck has not moved for 10 minutes, even with Bluetooth still connected**
Asked on 2026-10-06 with the finding above in front of him, Shawn chose: end the trip at the place and time it stopped once it has not moved for 10 minutes, and start a new trip if it moves again while still connected. This replaces his kickoff choice of "Bluetooth only" trip boundaries. He also asked that MilO "disconnects properly when not connected to the vehicle's Bluetooth": the rule makes the end of a trip independent of whether the phone reports the truck as connected.

**`[CHANGE]` The parked rule: a trip ends where the truck stopped, and MilO waits beside a truck that stays connected**
*This entry is the rule as it was first built. Three reviewers read it the same evening, and eight things in it were changed before it was merged: the `[FIX]` entry at the end of this date says which. Where the two disagree, that entry is what the code does.*
Built the same evening, from the decision above (ADR-002, amendment 28; APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, "Truck pairing and trip detection", rules 14 and 15). Both surfaces: on the phone Home's card, a row on Settings and the trip notification; on the Android Auto screen two more lines for the Status row, with no row added and no row title changed. **No table, no column, no migration (the main database is still version 5), no new value of a stored enum, no dependency, no permission and no manifest entry.** A trip that movement starts is stored as started by `TRUCK`, like any trip the truck starts, so the build before this one reads every row this one writes. Six keys are new in the settings file. Nothing under `platform/bluetooth/` was changed, and no rule about how a trip starts from a connect, the companion callback, a reconcile or a button, or about how a disconnect and the grace period end one.
- **The rule** (`core/trip/ParkedRules.kt`, called from `OpenTripRules`): a trip that is being recorded and whose last real movement is older than `TripRules.parkedLimitMs` is closed at that movement, on the next fresh reading of the truck. `TripRules.parkedLimitMs` has no value of its own in the rules: the trip controller passes the setting at every trigger (`TripRuleSettings`), 10 minutes out of the box. The 30-minute guard for manual trips, `NO_MOVEMENT_LIMIT_MS` and `noMovementGuardApplies` are gone; the reason a trip is closed for is still called `NO_MOVEMENT`. The two buttons moved to `ButtonRules.kt` unchanged, to keep `TripStateMachine.kt` under the size limit.
- **Movement** (`DistanceCalculator.kt`, `TripProgress.kt`): the time of a trip's last movement is now read from the distance calculation (the fix distance was last counted up to), so a step that the next fix takes back is taken back there too. The rules are told `Moved` for a fix that adds distance, as before, and the new `MoveTakenBack` when a step is taken back. Before, one stray fix moved a manual trip's no-movement limit on for good, and the trip was then cut at the stray fix.
- **The wait** (`TripState.parked`, the effects `StartWaiting` and `EndWaiting`, `StartTrip.fromParked`; `core/trip/ParkedWatch.kt`; `platform/trip/TripParking.kt`, `PickedUp.kt`): stored in the settings file with the place (`data/settings/ParkedStorage.kt`), watched by the distance calculation run over the place and the fixes of the wait, and ended by a trip whose first stored points are the place and the fixes that showed the truck leave.
- **The service** (`TripService`, `TripServiceLink`, `TripRecorder.watchParked`, `LocationRecorder` with `FixRate`, `TripNotifications`, `TripCheckTimer.kt`): a third order beside "record" and "stop". Recording and waiting share the session (the Bluetooth receiver, the minute check); waiting asks for a fix every 30 seconds in place of every 5, does not watch Android Auto, and shows "Truck connected and parked".
- **The screens:** `TripActivity.parked` (`ParkedTruckWatch`), Home's card, the Status row (`CarStatus.PARKED_WAITING`, `PARKED_NOT_WATCHED`), and the Settings row "End a trip after the truck has not moved for", 5 to 30 minutes in steps of 5 (`PARKED_LIMIT_CHOICE`).
- **The driving alert** (`DrivingAlertRules.kt`): one condition changed. It is now also shown for a truck that reads as connected when MilO has stopped watching it after 12 hours of waiting (`DRIVING_TRUCK_NOT_WATCHED`), because nothing else would start a trip then.
- **The event log:** the close ("…ended by NO_MOVEMENT (the truck was parked: it last moved at 17:08:12 and then stood still for 10 min)…"), the beginning of the wait, its end with the reason, and the trip that movement starts.
- **Tests:** 1,682 unit tests where there were 1,576. New: `TripStateMachineParkedTest` and `TripStateMachineParkedEdgesTest`, `TripStateMachineWaitingTest` and `TripStateMachineWaitingLimitTest`, `TripStateMachineParkedRandomTest`, `ParkedWatchTest`, `TripMovementTest`, `TripControllerParkedTest`, `TripControllerWaitingTest` and `TripControllerWaitingLimitTest`, `ParkedStorageTest`, and cases added to `CarScreenContentTest`, `TripActivityTest`, `SettingsUiStateTest` and `DrivingAlertRulesTest`.
- **Existing tests that were changed, and why.** Each asserted what this work replaces, except the first two.
  - `TripStateMachineRandomTest`: its stand-in for storage has a `when` over every effect, which must name the two new ones to compile. It now fails if either is ever asked for, since the parked rule is off in the rules that test runs with.
  - `TripControllerFakes` (`FakeService`) and `DrivingFixtures`: the stand-in service implements `watchParked`, and the fixture takes the alert's new fact. No assertion changed.
  - `TripStateMachineManualTest`: "a manual trip with no truck ends after 30 minutes without movement", "movement restarts the 30 minutes" and "End pressed on a forgotten manual trip…" now run with the parked limit (10 minutes) in place of the guard's 30. "The no-movement guard waits while Android Auto is connected" became "Android Auto does not hold a manual trip open that stands still": the opposite, as decided. "A truck trip is never ended for standing still" became "rules that set no parked limit never end a truck trip for standing still": it is true only of rules without the limit now, and the controller always sets one.
  - `TripStateMachineTest`: "for a manual trip with no truck the next look is 30 minutes after the last movement" and "a manual trip with no truck needs no timer while Android Auto is connected" became the same two statements about the parked limit: the timer is set for every recording trip, and Android Auto does not take it away.
  - `TripStateMachineRestoreTest`, "a forgotten manual trip is closed where it last moved": the same assertion, now under rules with the parked limit.
  - `TripControllerRulesTest` and `TripControllerReadingTest`, the two tests of a companion start that is confirmed: they asserted that a confirmed truck trip has no timer. It has one now, for the parked limit, and they assert its time.
  - `DrivingAlertQuietTest`, "a report just after End trip…": its trip stood still for 12 minutes before End was pressed, which is now a trip that ended at its start. The wait is 8 minutes.
  - `DrivingAlertRulesTest`, "only two verdicts show the alert": three.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes, with 1,682 tests and no lint finding.

**`[DECISION]` What was decided while building the parked rule, each Shawn's to change**
- **Time nobody watched counts as standing.** After a restart, a stored trip whose last movement is older than the limit is closed there, although the truck may have been driven while MilO's process was dead. If it was, the fixes that follow are far from the parked place, and a new trip starts from it: two trips where one drive was, and no distance lost. The other way round, a trip that really stood would record for ten more minutes after every restart.
- **A restart or a reconcile with the truck connected and no trip open starts a trip, as it always has,** and the parked rule closes it if the truck is standing: ten minutes of GPS and one discarded trip, in one line of the log. Going straight to waiting would lose the first minute of a drive already under way, and "MilO opened because a trip did not start" is exactly that case.
- **One fix every 30 seconds while waiting, high accuracy.** A cheaper kind of position (Wi-Fi, cell towers) would have to be let past the 25 m accuracy filter and would start trips on its own errors. 30 seconds means the next trip starts up to a minute after the truck drove off, from the parked place. **What it costs has not been measured;** the phone's own stationary detection may make it nearly free, or not (device check 271).
- **Two fixes before movement is believed.** Beside a parked truck, the first fix somewhere else may be one bad fix, and after hours of standing the speed check cannot tell. So the trip starts when the next fix bears it out.
- **12 hours of waiting, then MilO stops watching.** *(Replaced by the review: three days. See the `[FIX]` entry.)* The next trip then starts with a new link to the truck, a reconcile (MilO opened, a restart, a boot, an update), or Start; and the driving alert is shown in that state during the work hours. A truck that stays connected overnight and is driven off before anything else happens, outside the work hours, is not recorded. Whether this truck ever stays connected that long is not known.
- **The hold-off beats the wait.** End pressed while waiting sets the hold-off and ends the wait. A trip parked while the hold-off still stands (End, then Start, the truck connected throughout) ends without a wait, and the next drive needs Start. *(The second half was replaced by the review: such a trip gives the hold-off up and MilO waits.)*
- **A new link while waiting starts a trip at once,** with the sound, as every connect does: the proven path. The companion callback while waiting starts a trip that must be confirmed.
- **Waiting needs the truck's Bluetooth.** A trip that Android Auto alone held open is closed by the parked rule and nothing waits. *(Replaced by the review: Android Auto holds a wait as it holds a trip.)*
- **While waiting, the minute check goes on and amendment 18 holds for it:** a reading of "not connected" ends the wait only at the second in a row, and not at all while no reading has shown the truck on its present link.
- **The setting offers 5 to 30 minutes.** There is no "never": Shawn replaced "Bluetooth only".

**`[FINDING]` The timers of the parked rule would have ended every trip on a truck that no reading can see**
Found by a test while building. Until now a timer of the service ran only while the truck was believed gone (a grace period, a companion start to be confirmed, a manual trip with no truck). The parked limit is the first that runs while the truck is believed connected, and a timer tells the rules what its reading says. On a truck whose link is up but which is on neither profile a reading looks at (amendment 18), that reading is "not connected": ten minutes after such a truck stopped, the trip would have gone into its grace period and ended there, and with the link never dropping nothing would have started the next one. A timer's reading that proves nothing by amendment 18 is now set aside, and the timer goes by what is believed (`TripEvidence`, `CHECK_DUE`). This truck showed both profiles connected on 2026-10-06, so it is not the truck in question; the rule is kept whole all the same.

**`[FINDING]` The parked rule on an emulator: the close as built, the wait in a throwaway build**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, `emulator-5584`, read-only, without a window or sound, shut down afterwards; two other emulators and the phone were attached to the Mac, were not this work's and were not touched: every adb call went through one script pinned to that serial, which refuses a serial that is not an emulator's; builds signed with the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read). Positions were fed with `adb emu geo fix`, 100 m north every 2.5 seconds; the databases were read on the Mac from copies taken with `run-as`.
1. **The build of the gate, as it is.** Settings showed "End a trip after the truck has not moved for", "10 min"; one press of minus made it "5 min", and the minus was then greyed out. Start trip on Home at 18:59:30; 2 km of simulated driving; the last counted position at 19:00:27. Left alone. At 19:05:27 the log had "timer: the truck is not connected (no truck is paired)" and "Trip 1: finished, 1993 m; ended by NO_MOVEMENT (the truck was parked: it last moved at 19:00:27 and then stood still for 5 min); 18 fixes stored, 10 used, …", then "Location fixes stopped" and "Trip service destroyed". The trip row ends at 19:00:27 at the latitude of that fix; the notification was gone; Home said "No trip in progress" and listed the trip under Today. **One thing spoils the timing of this run:** the Mac's disk ran full while the trip stood, and the emulator stopped its virtual phone for about four minutes ("virtual device is stopped", resumed with `emu avd start`). Its clock was right again afterwards, so the five minutes are five minutes of the phone's clock and not of running time. Step 2 has the same close without that.
2. **The wait cannot run on an emulator as it is built:** it needs a truck that is believed connected, the shell is refused when it sends the link broadcast, and the image allows no root. So a second APK was built from a copy of this work in the scratch folder, with one change, `AppContainer.truckConnection` replaced by a stand-in that answers "connected" (and "not connected" while a file exists). The copy was deleted afterwards; nothing of it is in the change. Installed over the first build, with the same data:
   - **A trip the truck "started", closed where it stopped.** The update's reconcile started trip 2 at 19:07:14. 1 km driven; last movement 19:07:48. At 19:12:48, five minutes later to the second: "timer: the truck is connected (…)", "Trip 2: finished, 992 m; ended by NO_MOVEMENT (the truck was parked: it last moved at 19:07:48 and then stood still for 5 min); 66 fixes stored, 7 used, …", "Waiting for the truck to move: it is still connected. …", "Location fixes are now one every 30.0 s". The trip row ends at 19:07:48.
   - **The notification changed** from "Trip in progress, 1.0 km" to "Truck connected and parked", "A trip starts when the truck moves"; the service stayed in the foreground; Home's card said "Truck connected and parked" and "The last trip ended where the truck stopped. A new trip starts by itself when the truck moves."
   - **A dead process brings the wait back, not a trip.** `am crash` at 19:13:05. The new process: "process start: picked up the stored state; the truck is connected (…). Watching the parked truck needs the trip service, which is not running", the service in the foreground a second later (Android had restarted it as well), "Location fixes requested, one every 30.0 s", the notification still "Truck connected and parked", and still two trips.
   - **Driving off starts a trip at the parked place, without the sound.** Driving from 19:13:28. The watch's fixes at 19:13:40 and 19:14:10 both showed the truck gone, and at 19:14:10: "GPS: the truck moved", "No longer waiting for the truck to move: it moved", "Trip 3 started by TRUCK: it was connected and parked, and it moved. The trip starts where it was parked (3 points carried over), with no trip-start sound", "Location fixes are now one every 5.0 s". No sound line. Trip 3 is dated 19:13:40; its first stored point is trip 2's end position (53.5269249, with no speed), then the fixes of 19:13:40 and 19:14:10. The notification said "Trip in progress", and at the end of the 4 km drive "3.9 km": the stretch from the parked place to the first fix was counted.
   - **That trip was closed the same way, and holds the whole drive.** Last movement 19:15:11; at 19:20:11: "Trip 3: finished, 4007 m; ended by NO_MOVEMENT (the truck was parked: it last moved at 19:15:11 and then stood still for 5 min); 74 fixes stored, 15 used, …", and the wait began again. 40 positions 100 m apart were fed, so 4,000 m from the parked place. Trip 3's start position is trip 2's end position to the last digit.
   - **The truck gone ends the wait at the second reading.** With the stand-in answering "not connected" from 19:20:16: "minute check: the truck reads as not connected (…). One reading alone proves nothing" at 19:21:06, and at 19:22:06 "minute check: the truck is not connected (…)", "No longer waiting for the truck to move: it is no longer connected. Nothing was recorded", "Location fixes stopped", "Trip service destroyed". The notification was gone. Three trips in all; no point was stored for either wait; no `ERROR` line from any of it (the one of that run is from the first start of the process, where Play services did not answer the driving alert within 10 seconds on a phone that was still booting).
3. **Not seen anywhere:** the phone's real reading of a truck that stays connected; what HyperOS does to the service and its timers while MilO waits; the battery; the limit on waiting; the driving alert in that state; the Status row on a car; and the timer of the parked limit on a phone that sleeps.

**`[DEBT]` Corners cut in the parked rule**
- **The parked limit is not in the export file.** It is a setting that means the same on another phone, like the grace period, but the export's form is fixed and versioned, and adding a value needs a new format version and a reader for both. An import leaves the phone's own value. Android's backup carries it. Tagged in `data/settings/TransferStorage.kt`. Cost to fix: a version 2 of the format, half a day with its tests.
- **A walk is movement.** The distance calculation cannot tell a phone carried about from a truck driven, so a walk near the connected truck keeps a trip open and, beside a parked truck, starts one. The cure is the one already noted for the calculation: the phone's own speed reading, which is stored and not used. Device check 274 shows how much it matters.
- **If Android refuses the service for a wait** (a restart in the background on HyperOS), the warning is worded for a trip: "MilO could not start this trip. Tap to start". The wait stays stored and the next trigger tries again. A wording of its own would need a second notification.
- **"No longer watched" lives in memory only.** After the limit on waiting (12 hours as first built, three days since the review) the state is not stored, so a restart turns it into a plain restart with the truck connected, which starts a trip. That is the safe side.
- **Four existing tests now pass for a weaker reason than their names give.** They stand a truck trip still for hours and assert that nothing ends it; that holds only because the rules they run with set no parked limit. One was renamed to say so; "the truck connects during a manual trip…" and others like it were left as they are, because nothing they assert is false. A reader of those tests should know that `RULES` and `PARKED_RULES` differ in exactly this.

**`[FIX]` The review of the parked rule: eight ways the wait could lose the drive that follows it**
Three reviewers read the parked rule the evening it was built, before it was merged, and returned thirteen findings, three of them the same one. Each was looked at in the code and each was real. All are fixed here, and every change to the code has a unit test that fails without it; where a reviewer's own cure was not the one taken, the line says why. ADR-002, amendment 29, has the rules as they now stand. **Nothing about how a trip starts from a connect, the companion callback, a reconcile or a button was changed, nor how a disconnect and the grace period end one, nor anything under `platform/bluetooth/`.** No table, no column, no stored enum value, no dependency. **None of this pass ran on an emulator or the phone: unit tests only.**
- **The wait lasts three days, not 12 hours** (`WAITING_LIMIT_MS`, three reviewers, major). The 12 hours were justified as "longer than a working day", but what the limit has to outlast is a night at home. On 2026-10-06 the truck was driven off at 07:18 and parked at 17:08; a night between those two times is 14 hours 10 minutes. The wait would begin at 17:18 and stop at 05:18; if the truck stays connected overnight (not known), a commute like that morning's 41.5 km would start nothing, with no new link, and no driving alert before the work hours. 72 hours covers a Friday evening to a Monday morning (62 hours). **A judgement, Shawn's to change.** Its price is up to three days of a GPS fix every 30 seconds, whose cost is still unmeasured (device check 271). Not done instead: a slower watch after the limit, which needs a third rate, a second kind of wait and its own screens; and letting a report of driving read the truck, which amendment 27 forbids by construction.
- **End, then Start, then a stop: the hold-off is given up and MilO waits** (`ParkedRules.closeIfParked`, the new `HoldOffRelease.TRIP_PARKED`; major). As built, a trip parked while the hold-off stood ended with nothing waiting: the service stopped, the hold-off stayed, and the next drive was recorded by nothing, silently, because the truck read as connected. Before the parked rule that trip simply stayed open. The reviewer's fix was to release the hold-off at the press of Start. It is released at the parked close instead, which reaches the same end and leaves the Start button's step exactly as it was: an existing test of the button (`manual start with the truck connected starts a trip that ends like an automatic one`) asserts that step effect for effect. The one difference between the two: a trip started by hand while held off whose process dies for more than 30 minutes is closed as stale at the restart, and the hold-off then still keeps a connected truck from starting a trip.
- **Android Auto holds a wait** (`ParkedRules`, `TripService.turnTo`; major). A trip that Android Auto alone was holding (Bluetooth down, the phone on the cable: the case ADR-002 names) was closed by the parked rule and nothing waited, so the drive after a ten-minute stop was lost until Bluetooth came back. Before the parked rule it was recorded. Now MilO waits when either is connected, the service keeps its watch on Android Auto through the wait, and the wait ends when both are gone. Two limits, both in APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA: such a wait does not survive a restart of the process, and once MilO has stopped watching Android Auto holds nothing.
- **A new link that finds a trip parked starts the next one in the same step** (`TripStateMachine.step`; minor). Stand nine minutes, lose the truck, get it back ninety seconds later: one delivery of the link broadcast closed the trip and MilO waited in silence; two deliveries, which is the normal case during a trip, ended that wait and started a trip with the sound. `step` promises that the same event twice changes nothing. Now the first delivery closes the parked trip and starts the new one, and a test runs every event twice from every state with the parked rule on.
- **An unreadable truck at a process start does not end a wait** (`storedWaitStandsUnread` in `PickedUp.kt`, `TripEvidence.waitCarriedOnUnread`; minor). The profile proxies get three seconds; if they miss them at a restart, the reading is "unknown", which counted as "not connected" and ended the wait for good, with a log line saying "it is no longer connected", which nothing had read. The wait now carries on, the log says so, and the readings that follow decide.
- **A first fix waits two minutes for a second, not for ever** (`ParkedWatch`, `MOVEMENT_BORNE_OUT_WITHIN_MS`; minor). The reviewer's case was one fix 60 m off, fifteen minutes of unusable fixes, then driving: a trip dated fifteen minutes back and closed at once as parked. Looking at it showed a worse one: a phone indoors overnight gives mostly unusable fixes, so one displaced fix in the evening would have been borne out by the first good fix of the morning, and the trip dated the evening before.
- **The trip that movement starts is told when it last moved** (`TripWorker.onFix`; minor). It was told only of the first of the fixes that showed the truck leave. A truck that stopped again at once (150 m, then 450 m, then parked) was cut at the first: 150 m, under the minimum, discarded, where it should be 450 m and kept.
- **A trip that never moved is waited for at its newest usable fix** (`ParkedWatch.placeAfter`; minor). Such a trip (a warm-up, or the trip a reconcile starts beside a standing truck) is cut at its start and has no end position, so the wait had no place and its first fix became one. A reviewer drove off within 30 seconds of the close on an emulator and lost 350 m.
- **The first points of that trip are the watch's own** (`TripParking`; minor). `ParkedWatch.tripStart` was tested and not used: the stored points came from a second copy of the rule. They are now built from it.
- **Tests: 1,720 where there were 1,682,** counted before this work was put on top of the daily "nothing recorded" check. With that check's 64 tests the gate runs 1,784: see the first entry of this date. New: `TripStateMachineParkedHeldTest`, `TripControllerWaitingEdgesTest`, `TripControllerWaitingServiceTest`, `DrivingAlertParkedTest`, `FixRateTest`, and cases added to `TripStateMachineParkedEdgesTest`, `TripStateMachineWaitingLimitTest`, `ParkedWatchTest` and `SettingsViewModelTest`. One reviewer had changed ten lines of the code one at a time and found the suite still green. Each of the ten now has a test written against it: the service lost and the service refused in the middle of a wait, a fix standing in for the timer of the waiting limit and being looked at before it, the driving alert reading "no longer watched" from the controller, the watch's fix rate, the limit waiting for a reading of the truck, the edge of the limit at a restart, the hold-off beside a wait, and the direction of the Settings row. One reservation: that last one is tested in the view model. Which way round the two buttons are wired in the Composable cannot be, because there are no Compose tests (STANDARDS §11); device check 265 is that test.
- **Tests of this work package that were changed, and why.** No test that was on `main` before the parked rule was touched in this pass except the last one named here. `TripStateMachineParkedEdgesTest`, "a trip parked while automatic start is held off ends, and nothing waits": the opposite is now asserted. `TripStateMachineParkedTest`, "a parked trip that only Android Auto held open ends, and nothing waits": moved to the new test class with the opposite outcome, and its place taken by a trip with nothing connected. `TripStateMachineParkedRandomTest`: a wait may now be held by Android Auto, and a restart is given the wait only while it is watched, as storage is. `TripControllerParkedTest` and the two "twelve hours" tests: the limit by its constant and without its old figure. `TripStateMachineManualTest`, "Android Auto does not hold a manual trip open that stands still" (rewritten by the first pass from the 30-minute guard's test): it now also asserts the wait that follows.
- **The documents:** ADR-002 (amendment 29, and the passages of amendment 28 it replaces), APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (rules 10, 13, 14 and 15, the gotchas, and three stale passages a reviewer named), ARCHITECTURE, `README.md` (the owner's manual knew only the disconnect, said the truck had never been met, and counted two trip numbers), and device checks 281 to 285.

**`[DEBT]` What the review of the parked rule left**
- **Up to three days of the watch, unmeasured.** The limit was raised on the ground that a missed commute is worse than a drained battery, without knowing what the watch costs. Device check 271 is one night of it. If the cost is high, the cure is the slower watch the reviewers suggested, not a shorter limit.
- **A wait that Android Auto alone holds ends at a restart of the process,** because Android Auto cannot be read before the service is up. Cost to fix: store what held the wait and bring the service up to ask, half a day with its tests.
- **While MilO waits, a change of Android Auto is logged twice,** by the trip controller and by the app-wide watch, which goes by "no trip is being recorded". Harmless; one condition in `app/CarObjects.kt` would end it.
- **After Android has refused the service for a wait, Home keeps the card that says a start failed** until a trip next opens, although the wait is watched again once the service is back. The card is cleared only by a trip.
- **If the timer of the waiting limit is slept through and the truck drives off in that moment,** the first fix that shows it leaving is followed by the late timer's reading, which stops the watch before a second fix can bear the first out. Three days and ten seconds after a close.
- **The device checks for this pass are thin where the phone is hardest to steer:** nothing on the phone can make Bluetooth unreadable at a restart on demand, so that fix rests on unit tests alone.

### 2026-10-06 (evening, decisions)

**`[DECISION]` Four open decisions settled by Shawn on 2026-10-06**
Asked one by one that evening, after the first day in the truck. His answers:
- **The trip-start sound always plays,** at alarm volume, even when the phone is on silent, vibrate, Do Not Disturb or Bedtime mode. This replaces his kickoff choice ("respects silent"). The reason is in the log of 2026-10-05: with the phone in Bedtime mode he heard nothing and took it for a fault.
- **Take the Car App Library's pre-release now** for its security fix, as a one-time exception to the stable-only rule, and return to the stable line when it is released. The exception belongs in ADR-001.
- **Add the "nothing recorded" check** from the list of extras: on a tracked day, if no trip has been recorded by a set time, MilO tells him. None of the other extras were chosen.
- **Support Android 14 and newer only.** His phone is on Android 14 and will not be updated; a future phone will be newer. The branches written for Android 12 and 13 can never be tested on it and are to be removed. This replaces the brief's minSdk 31.
Still open: nothing. The Android Auto screen's fate waits on whether it appears on the truck.

**`[FIX]` The review of the daily check and of the library update: four findings, all of them real; one leaves a question for Shawn**
Reviewers read the change below before it was committed: one major finding and three minor ones. All four were acted on. Nothing under `platform/trip/`, `platform/bluetooth/` or `core/trip/` was changed for them, no table, no permission, no dependency.
- **The check could say "No trip recorded today" a moment before a trip began (major).** It took the trip controller's "I have caught up" to mean that a trip which is being started is stored. It does not mean that. The controller opens a trip only once the trip service is in the foreground (ADR-002); between asking Android for the service and the service reporting in, it holds no trip row, publishes none and has nothing left to do, so it answers at once. A look in that gap read no trip, posted the notification with its sound, stored the day as shown, and had the notification taken away a fraction of a second later. The gap is hit when MilO is opened beside a connected truck, and can be hit when the truck's connect, a reboot or an update starts MilO's process, on any work day whose notification has not come yet. A reviewer reproduced it beside the real controller. **Now "no trip" is said only by a second look:** a look that finds a notification due waits 2.5 seconds (`TRIP_START_WAIT_MS`), has the controller catch up once more (the service may have reported in, with its trip still waiting in the controller's inbox), reads the settings and the trips again, and posts only if the answer is still the same. One line is written, the second look's; where a trip came in the wait it ends "A first look, a moment earlier, found no trip and waited before notifying: one was just being started." Every other answer is acted on at once, as before. **Why a wait and not a signal:** nothing the check is handed says that a start is pending, and the controller was not to change. **What it costs:** the notification comes about three seconds after the look that brings it; and a look at a process start that a reboot or an update caused has nothing holding MilO awake for those seconds (the alarm's look is held by its broadcast, an opened MilO by its screen), so a phone that puts MilO to sleep inside them delays that look until MilO next runs. Not seen; ARCHITECTURE has it among the things only the phone can show. **What is left:** a trip service that takes longer than 2.5 seconds to come up still gets the notification, which the trip takes away as it begins. The 2.5 seconds are a judgement; how long the phone takes has not been measured (device check NR-15, which also measures it). A controller that did not answer the first time is not waited for a second time, so the five seconds and the wait together stay under the eight a broadcast is held open for.
- **Four sentences said the opposite of the code,** and are corrected: the comment on the wait in the check, the comment in `MainActivity.onStart` ("a trip that the reconcile starts … is found by it"), step 6 of the encyclopedia's entry, and the bullet "It waits for the trip controller" in ARCHITECTURE, which now has a second bullet for what "caught up" does not mean.
- **`NothingRecordedCheck.kt` would have passed 300 lines,** so one look is now a class of its own, `NothingRecordedLook.kt` (the wait for the controller, the second look, the lock, showing and withdrawing, storing the day). The check keeps when it looks, the alarm and the two watches. What is handed to the check from outside (`CheckObjects`) is unchanged.
- **The Dependabot rule for the Car App Library failed unsafe (minor).** It hid everything above 1.8.0 (`"> 1.8.0"`) and had to be removed by hand in the change that takes 1.8.0. Dependabot's own pull request for 1.8.0 changes the version catalog only, and is merged when CI is green: the rule would have stayed, and 1.8.1, 1.9.0 and every later release, security fixes included, would never have been offered again, with nothing to show for it. **The rule now names pre-releases only:** `(1.8.0-rc01,1.8.0)`, a later release candidate of 1.8.0, and `[1.9.0-alpha01,1.9.0)`, every pre-release of 1.9.0. Neither range holds a stable release, and Dependabot offers no pre-release for a library that is on a stable version, so a rule that is forgotten hides nothing. Read again from `dependabot-core` on 2026-10-06 (the Gradle version finder, and the Maven range parser it shares: `(a,b)` is "above a and below b", `[a,b)` is "a or above, and below b"); **still not seen running.** Not hidden: a pre-release of a version after 1.9.0, of which there is none today. The file, the version catalog, ADR-001 and ENGINEERING_STANDARDS section 2 say what the rule is now, and the standards have the lesson as a rule: such a rule names ranges of pre-releases, never "everything above".
- **Open, for Shawn: should there be a Dependabot rule for this library at all?** Nobody asked for one; it was added because, without it, every weekly pull request of minor updates would carry a 1.9.0 alpha of this library for as long as the release candidate is pinned, and that pull request is the one that is merged when CI is green. The choice: **keep the rule** (as it is now: it can be forgotten safely), or **have none** and take that library's line out of each weekly pull request by hand until 1.8.0 is stable. It was left in, in its safe form, until he says.
- **A change of the work schedule did not move the check's alarm (minor).** The alarm is asked for at the later of the time set and the next day's start, so it depends on the schedule, and only the check's own card, a process start, an import and the alarm itself asked for it again. With the time set to 07:00 and Wednesday starting at 10:00, moving Wednesday's start to 08:00 on Tuesday evening left the alarm at 10:00; and a day switched off in the schedule kept its notification until the next look. With the settings out of the box (noon, 08:00) nothing was affected. **The check now follows the stored schedule by itself:** it watches the settings store's flow, and a change of the schedule has it ask for the alarm again and look, with "(the work schedule was changed)" in its two lines. **Why in the check and not in the Settings screen's ViewModel:** that file is at its size limit and was being changed by another piece of work at the same time, and the watch also covers a schedule that an import replaces. A settings file that cannot be read ends the watch quietly; the look says that itself.
- **No test pinned "one look at a time" (minor).** Taking the lock away left every test green: the stand-in for the settings file answered at once, so two looks could never overlap. `NothingRecordedCheckWaitTest` now has a settings file whose reads take a moment, and two looks at the same moment (the process start and the alarm that started the process): one notification and one "shown" line.
- **Tests:** 12 new, 64 for the check in all. `NothingRecordedWithControllerTest` (5, beside a real trip controller: MilO opened beside the connected truck; a process the truck started; a service that reports in at the very moment the wait ends; one that takes longer than the wait; no truck and no trip) and `NothingRecordedCheckWaitTest` (7: nothing said or written before the second look; a trip stored in the wait and the line for it; no second look for any other answer; the lock; the schedule's three). The 19 tests of the check that were there are unchanged in what they ask; where a notification is due they now let the wait pass. **Each change was then taken out again on purpose, one at a time, and tests failed every time:** the wait, the lock, the second catch-up, a second wait for a controller that does not answer, the watch on the schedule, its first value, the sentence about the wait, and a wait of half a second.
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug`, run from scratch after these changes with a fresh JVM (`--no-daemon --rerun-tasks --no-build-cache`), passes with 1,640 unit tests (1,628 before) and no lint finding. Gradle's one deprecation notice is AGP's own, as before.
- **Not run on an emulator or on the phone:** the two changes to the check. Device checks NR-15 (the truck, and the time the trip service takes) and NR-16 (the schedule).

**`[CHANGE]` Two of those decisions built: the daily "nothing recorded" check, and the Car App Library's release candidate**
Phone only: a notification and a Settings card. No table, no migration, no permission and no new dependency. Nothing under `core/trip/`, `platform/trip/` or `platform/bluetooth/` changed: how a trip starts, records and ends is what it was. Device checks NR-1 to NR-14, in a section of their own.
- **The check** (`platform/nothingrecorded/`). On a work day (a day the schedule tracks), from the time set (12:00 out of the box; from the day's own start if that is later), if no trip was started that day, MilO posts one notification: "No trip recorded today" / "That is fine if the truck has not been driven today. If it has, tap to open MilO and check that it is still set up." One a day at most. A tap opens Home. A trip that starts afterwards takes it away at once. The decision is the pure function `judgeNothingRecorded`.
- **Built like the monthly reminder:** decided by what is true when MilO looks; looked at by one inexact alarm a day (no permission, no exact-alarm permission, no WorkManager), at every process start, every time MilO comes to the front, when its card is changed and after an import. It is handed the settings store, two reads of the stored trips, what the trip controller publishes and the controller's "tell me when you have caught up": neither the controller nor a way to write a trip. Every piece of its work is caught (`NothingRecordedFailures`), as in the driving alert.
- **Files.** New: `platform/nothingrecorded/` (seven files: rules, check, failures, alarm, receiver, notification, log wording), `data/settings/NothingRecordedStorage.kt`, `feature/settings/NothingRecordedCard.kt` and `NothingRecordedViewModel.kt`, `app/CheckObjects.kt`, `res/values/strings_nothing_recorded.xml`, and eight test files (two of them fixtures that tests share). **Shared files touched, each as little as it took:** `MiloSettings.kt` (one field), `SettingsStore.kt` (one line that reads it), `SettingsScreen.kt` (the card as a slot, after the driving alert's), `MiloNavigation.kt` (its ViewModel), `AppContainer.kt` (one line, `checks`), `TransferObjects.kt` (one line after an import), `MiloApplication.kt` and `MainActivity.kt` (the look at a start and at the front; the tap), `MiloApp.kt` (Home for the tap), and the manifest (one receiver). `strings.xml` was not touched: another change to the Settings screen was being made beside this one, so the check's words went into a file of their own.
- **The event log:** `TRIP` lines that begin "Nothing-recorded", no new category: the alarm asked for or taken back, **one line for every asking with what was decided** (the reminder writes only when its answer changes; here the line is the proof that MilO looked), and one when the notification is shown.
- **Tests:** 52 new (`NothingRecordedRulesTest` 14, `NothingRecordedClockTest` 6, `NothingRecordedCheckTest` 12, `NothingRecordedCheckFailureTest` 7, `NothingRecordedStorageTest` 7, `NothingRecordedViewModelTest` 6). Three of the rules were then broken on purpose (the stroke of the set time, the hand-added trip, the trip that is being recorded) and the tests failed, 15 of them.
- **The library.** `androidx.car.app` (`app` and `app-projected`) moved from 1.7.0 to **1.8.0-rc01**. The live Maven metadata was read first: there is no stable 1.8.0 (the list ends `1.7.0, 1.8.0-alpha01 … 1.8.0-rc01, 1.9.0-alpha01, 1.9.0-alpha02`), so the exception was needed, and rc01 is the newest release candidate of 1.8.0. **No Kotlin file, resource or manifest entry changed for it:** the gate passed at once. ADR-001 has the exception, with its reason and its end ("Exception, 2026-10-06").
- **Gate:** `spotlessCheck lintDebug testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` passes, run from scratch at the end, with 1,628 unit tests and no lint finding. It was first run with the library alone (1,576 tests, as before) and then with the check. Gradle's one deprecation notice is AGP's own, as before.
- **Never run on the phone:** all of it. The alarm reaching MilO under HyperOS, with Autostart on and off, is the question that decides whether the check can do what it is for (NR-9 to NR-11).

**`[DECISION]` Points the work order did not settle, decided while building the check**
All of them are Shawn's to change.
- **A trip that was started counts, whatever became of it:** being recorded, finished, discarded, or deleted by him afterwards, and whether the truck or a Start button started it. The question is "was a trip started today?", and each of those was. Only a trip added by hand does not count, as he was told.
- **A trip that is being recorded counts even if it began before midnight.** Otherwise MilO would say "no trip recorded today" beside its own "Trip in progress".
- **An edited trip goes by the start MilO recorded,** so a trip moved into today by hand does not hide a day without detection. The other way round is not caught: a trip of today whose start was moved to another day is no longer found (the trips are looked up by their stored start; the other lookup would need a new query in `TripDao`, which is at its size limit).
- **The check runs from the time set until midnight,** not only inside the work hours. A phone that was off until the evening still says it once.
- **The alarm wakes the phone (`RTC_WAKEUP`); the reminder's does not (`RTC`).** This notification is worth most while the work day is still going on, and a phone that sleeps in the truck would otherwise show it when it is next picked up. One wake a day, and none while the check is switched off.
- **Default importance, on a channel of its own ("Daily check"):** the phone's notification sound, no pop-up. On a work day without a drive before the time set it is a false alarm, and a false alarm must not be loud. He can change the channel in the phone's settings.
- **A look waits for the trip controller, for five seconds at most,** and then looks anyway and says so. The wait is what keeps a trip the last process left open from counting; the limit is there because this check is for the day something in MilO does not work.
- **With settings that cannot be read, nothing is checked,** as in the reminder (it would otherwise notify at every opening of MilO, with no way to remember that it had). The driving alert goes the other way and runs on the defaults.
- **A day that is not a work day is looked at too,** at the time set: that look takes away a notification left from the day before and keeps the alarm going.
- **Moving the time later after the day's notification takes it away for that day.** "One a day" was put before "show it again at the new time".
- **The time has no range:** any minute of the day, picked on the clock dial the work schedule uses. A time before a day's start is accepted and means "from the start".
- **The card stands under the driving alert,** and has a ViewModel of its own, as the last card has: the screen's is at its size limit.
- **The tap leads to Home by what the bar's Home button does** (`showTopLevel`), so the question about unsaved work comes first there too.

**`[CHANGE]` What the Car App Library's release candidate changed, as far as it could be read**
The two AARs were taken apart and compared, 1.7.0 against 1.8.0-rc01, and the release notes of 1.8.0-alpha01 to rc01 read.
- **Nothing MilO uses was removed or deprecated.** The notes add a media category, new templates and row images, and deprecate one method of the hardware API (`Mileage#getOdometerMeters`), which MilO does not call. The classes the Android Auto screen is built from (`Pane`, `PaneTemplate`, `Header`, their builders, the row constraints) and the classes behind `CarConnection` hold the same code in both versions; `Row` and `Action` gained members and lost none. The highest Car API level is 8 in both. The library's manifest adds no permission (its lowest Android version moved from 5.0 to 6.0), and the list of Android Auto's signing certificates is unchanged. It brings Guava 32.0.1 where 1.7.0 brought 31.1.
- **The answer "type 0" still does not show that the Android Auto app answered.** `CarConnectionTypeLiveData` is unchanged: a missing, an empty and an unreadable answer are each handed on as 0. So the entry of this morning stands for the new version too.
- **One change was found in the check of who may connect,** the code MilO's exported car service depends on. In 1.7.0, `HostValidator` lets in a host that holds `android.car.permission.TEMPLATE_RENDERER`, on any device, beside the hosts on its list. In 1.8.0-rc01 it does that only where the device is a car that runs Android itself; on a phone only the listed hosts are let in. Google does not say that this is the security fix its notes mention, and nothing else was found that could be.
- **Seen on an emulator with the new library** (Android 16): the watch on Android Auto outside trips wrote its first reading at both process starts of the first session, and the trip service's own watch wrote its line at a trip start, all "type 0" beside the emulator's stub of the Android Auto app, as with 1.7.0. The Android Auto screen itself has still run nowhere.
