- **Why it was left:** the package was to start the trip through the existing tap-to-start path and to leave how a trip starts alone, and each way out changes a decision that is Shawn's. (1) Aim the tap at a small receiver that hands `MANUAL_START` to the trip controller, as Home's button does. The preflight then runs and a failure brings the "could not start" warning with its reason in the log. But the one tap then depends on Android, and HyperOS, letting a receiver that a notification started bring up the location service from the background, which nothing has shown on the phone; the direct start is the case Android documents. And the preflight is deliberately stricter than Android: it also refuses without the Bluetooth permission and without "Allow all the time", where a direct start may well record a usable trip (not tried). (2) Post no alert while the preflight finds a problem. That silences the alert on a phone without the Bluetooth permission, which is the case judgement 1 below wants it for.
- **Cost to fix later:** small either way, after the decision. Tagged `// TODO(debt):` in `TripNotifications.showDrivingAlert`. Listed under the gotchas of the encyclopedia's "Safety net".

**`[CHANGE]` The driving alert (the third piece of phase 2)**
Phone only: a notification, one Setup row, one Settings switch. Its rules run in unit tests; the registration, the row and the switch ran on an emulator (the finding below); **no report of driving had reached MilO anywhere when this was written, so the notification had never been posted or tapped** (what the reviewers saw afterwards is in the entry above). Device checks DA-1 to DA-12, and DA-13 and DA-14 since the review. **This entry describes the package as first built; the review above changed three things in it, each marked here.**
- **What it does.** During the work hours, when the phone's driving detection reports that it has entered a vehicle while no trip is open and the truck is not known to be connected, MilO posts "You seem to be driving" / "No trip is being recorded. Tap to start one." on a new high-importance channel, "Driving alert".
- **It never starts a trip.** Only the tap does, and through the path the "could not start this trip" notification already uses: a `PendingIntent` for the trip service carrying `MANUAL_START`. The two taps are told apart by the intent's request number, which is why the existing one now passes 0 by name; with one number for both, Android would have replaced the first notification's intent with the second's, and a tap on either would have been logged as the other. The class that judges a report (`DrivingAlert`) is given neither the trip controller nor the trip repository, and a unit test runs it beside a real controller and checks that no report asks for the trip service or opens a trip.
- **The rule** (`judgeDriving`, pure, `platform/driving/`): the newest report is "entered" and at most 5 minutes old; the switch is on; no trip is open in storage; a fresh reading of the truck is not "connected"; and `classifyTrip` would make a trip that starts now Business. (Since the review above also: a truck is paired, no trip ended in the last 5 minutes, and no alert was posted in the last 30.) Using the schedule's own rule keeps "the work hours" the same here as on the Trips screen, and `core/schedule/` unchanged.
- **Where it comes from.** Play services' Activity Recognition Transition API, `IN_VEHICLE` entering and leaving, delivered through a mutable `PendingIntent` that names `DrivingReceiver`, a manifest receiver that is not exported. It is part of `play-services-location`, which the build already had: **no dependency was added.** One permission was: `ACTIVITY_RECOGNITION` ("Physical activity").
- **A new way for the system to start MilO** (ADR-002, amendment 27). A report can start a dead process, if HyperOS lets it. That process start runs the reconcile like any other, so with the truck really connected a trip then starts by the existing rule; the report itself starts nothing.
- **The request to the phone** is made at every process start, which renews it after a reboot and an update (both start a process), looked at again whenever MilO comes to the front, and changed at once when the Settings switch is pressed. The phone is asked only when the switch or the permission has changed since this process last asked.
- **Setup:** a tenth Android row, "Physical activity", **recommended**, not required. **Settings:** a sixth card, "Driving alert", with the switch "Tell me when I drive without a trip", on out of the box.
- **The event log** has a new category, `DRIVING`: a line for every delivery of reports with what was done and why, the facts in its detail, and a line whenever what MilO asks of the phone changes.
- **Storage:** one new key in the settings file, `driving_alert_enabled` (and since the review above a second, `last_driving_alert_at_ms`). **No table, no column and no database version changed;** `app/schemas/` is untouched.
- **Changed under `platform/trip/`: `TripNotifications.kt` only,** for the third notification: its channel, `showDrivingAlert`, `cancelDrivingAlert`, and `startTrip` taking the request number and the source it used to hard-code. `TripService`, the controller, the worker, the ledger, the starter and the intent helper are untouched. **Under `platform/bluetooth/` and `core/trip/`: nothing.** The boot and update receiver was left alone on purpose: the process start it causes is what renews the request, and a second call from the receiver would have asked for what had just been asked. A trip that starts takes the alert away without the trip service knowing of it: the alert follows what the controller publishes.
- **Design system:** nothing new. The row is a `StatusRow` like the others; the card is a `SectionCard` with a `SwitchRow` and two quiet lines.
- **Tests:** 47 new, 819 in all. The rule: every reason for and against, which report of a delivery counts, the 5-minute limit, the edges of the work hours, an untracked day, the time zone (17). The alert beside a real trip controller on stand-ins: nothing is ever started, what each report leads to, a trip left open by the last process, what is asked of the phone and when, a refusal logged once (16). The log lines (7). The Setup row and who is warned (5), the switch on the Settings screen (1) and in the settings file (1).

**`[DECISION]` Seven judgements made while building the driving alert, all Shawn's to change**
The kickoff answers settled the surface and that the alert only notifies. These were not settled and were decided so the work could go on:
1. **An unreadable truck shows the alert.** The trip rules never act on "unknown" (ADR-002, amendment 11). Here the worst it can cause is one notification, and a phone that cannot read Bluetooth is exactly the phone on which no trip starts by itself. That is about a paired truck. **With no truck paired there is no alert** (as first built there was one; the review above).
2. **A report of entering a vehicle more than 5 minutes old is ignored** (`MAX_DRIVING_REPORT_AGE_MS`). Nothing promises that a report is fresh: each carries the time the phone noticed it, a delivery can hold several, and MilO asks to be told again at every process start. (The assistant recalls the API's reference saying that a delivery "may include events in the past"; the page could not be opened to check on 2026-10-06, so that is not a source.) Without a limit, opening MilO at a desk could bring up an alert about the morning's drive. Too short a limit would drop a real report that reached a process HyperOS had to wake first; every ignored report is logged with its age, so the phone will show whether 5 minutes is right.
3. **A report of leaving the vehicle takes the alert away.** "Tap to start a trip" is wrong once the drive is over. The price: the phone can report "left" at a stop, and a new "entered" then finds the alert gone. As first built it was posted anew each time, with its sound; since the review above there is no second alert within 30 minutes, so after such a stop the alert stays away for the rest of the half hour.
4. **The alert is switched on out of the box.** The brief asks for it, and it does nothing until the permission is granted.
5. **The Physical activity row is recommended, not required.** A row is required "if an automatic trip can fail to start, or be cut short, without it", and that is not true of this permission. Required, it would keep "Setup needs attention" on the home screen for a safety net. With the alert switched off the row is in order and asks for nothing.
6. **"Is a trip being recorded?" is answered by the stored open trip, after the controller has caught up,** not by what the controller publishes. A report can be what started the process. A trip the last process left open is then published only once the trip service is up again, seconds later, and an alert in between would be wrong; a trip left open long ago has been closed by the controller's first look at storage by the time the alert asks. A consequence: an open trip that Android refuses to resume counts as a trip, and the "could not start this trip" notification is the one that speaks.
7. **The Settings switch tells the alert at once;** every other setting is simply read at the next event. The alert has no event of its own to read it at: it has to ask the phone to report driving, or to stop.

**`[FINDING]` The driving alert on an emulator: the request is accepted, and nothing can make an emulator report driving**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, Google Play services 26.31.31, `emulator-5740`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it through one script that refuses any other serial, shut down afterwards; the build signed with the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read).
- **A fresh install, opened:** no crash. `DRIVING` "Driving alert: not watching for driving (process start): the Physical activity permission is not granted".
- **Setup:** the tenth row, "Physical activity", red, "Recommended. The driving alert needs this: …", with "Allow". The button brought up Android's dialog "Allow MilO to access your physical activity?". After Allow the row said "Allowed." without the screen being left, and the log had "Driving alert: watching for driving (MilO in front)": **Play services accepted the request**, with the mutable `PendingIntent` that names the receiver (`dumpsys activity intents` lists it for `.platform.driving.DrivingReceiver`).
- **Settings:** the card "Driving alert" between "Trips outside the work hours" and "Trip-start sound", its switch on. Switched off: "…not watching for driving (the Settings switch): it is switched off in Settings", and no `ERROR` line, so the request to stop was accepted too. Switched on again: "…watching for driving (the Settings switch)".
- **The process killed in the background and MilO opened again:** "Driving alert: watching for driving (process start)".
- **The channel** "Driving alert" exists with importance high, beside the two older ones.
- **Not seen, anywhere:** a report arriving at the receiver; the notification; its tap starting a trip; the alert being taken away. An emulator has no way to report "in a vehicle", and the receiver is not exported, so nothing outside MilO can stand in for Play services. All of it rests on unit tests of the decision and on the code. Play services handed over no report when the request was made, so whether it hands over old ones on this phone is still open (judgement 2 above).
- **Not seen on the phone:** any of it. Above all whether a report can start MilO's process on HyperOS with Autostart off, the open question of ADR-002's "Deferred" list, and whether HyperOS lets the notification make a sound.

**`[FIX]` The review of the work schedule: five findings, four acted on, one left for Shawn**
Reviewers read the package below before it was committed and made five findings, one of them major. Each is listed with what was done. No table, no migration and no setting changed, so the emulator proof of the migration below stands as it is. No library was added.
- **A refused time could not be seen (major).** The red line for hours that do not end after they start was drawn once, at the top of the Work schedule card. The card is taller than the screen, so for Friday the line was some 1,700 px above the button that was pressed: the dial closed, the time stayed, and nothing on the screen said why. The line now stands **directly under the two times of the day it is about**, and the screen moves up until all of it can be read (`BringIntoViewRequester`, as for the Delete button on Trips). Which day it stands under is decided in the pure function (`ScheduleDay.hoursRefused` in `settingsUiState`); the ViewModel keeps the day the press was about beside the problem. Two details that are not obvious from the code. The screen moves once for every time the picker gives, so a second refusal for the same day, which changes nothing in the state, still brings the line back into view. And only a press moves it: the count of the picker's answers is not saved, so a line drawn again after the phone was turned leaves the screen where it is.
- **The time picker ignored the phone's own 24-hour switch.** The dial and every printed time went by the language alone, a decision of the entry below, taken so that the dial and the screen agree. On a phone set to 24 hours in English the dial then had AM and PM, and a missed tap on one of them stores a time that is valid and wrong, which decides Business or Personal. **Reversed:** the phone's switch ("Use 24-hour format") now decides, read in one place (`rememberTwentyFourHourClock` in the design system, at each resume). The dial and the screen still agree, because **every time of day on the phone screens follows the same switch**: a day's two times, and a trip's times on Home, on Trips and in the question before a delete. Changing only the Settings screen would have left Settings and Trips writing times differently on the same phone. `formatTimeOfDay` and `formatClockTime` are told the switch and stay pure: they use the language's own short form where it already has that clock, as on a phone whose switch follows its language, so nothing changes there; only where the two disagree they write "16:30" or "4:30 PM". The language's own habit is now read from the hour letter of its pattern, not from its AM or PM marker, which some languages replace with another letter. The Log's times and the Android Auto screen are untouched.
- **Device check 133 could not give the result it expected.** "Tap Monday's End and choose 6:00" is 6:00 in the evening on a dial that opens on PM, and that is a valid end. The check now says the time of day in words, says to tap AM or PM first and why, and says what a missed tap looks like. Checks 130, 131 and 134 name both ways a time can be written.
- **`core/schedule/` was not pure Kotlin.** `TripCategoryWords.kt` imported the Android resource class there, against STANDARDS §3, ARCHITECTURE section 5 and the encyclopedia. The file and its test moved to `core/designsystem/text/`, a new sub-package for words two features must say alike. The function, its name and its three tests are unchanged. No file under `core/trip/`, `core/schedule/` or `core/util/` imports anything of Android.
- **A trip is sorted in the time zone the phone is in when it ends. Not changed.** A reviewer reproduced it: a trip started at 08:23 on a tracked Tuesday, the zone changed during the trip, and it was stored as Personal. On the road that is a work drive from Alberta into the Pacific zone, or in winter into Saskatchewan late in the day; with "Ignore them" on, the trip is left out. The cure is to store the zone with the trip at its start, which is a new column and a change to how a trip is started, and this package may not touch the start. The decision below stands, and the encyclopedia now says what it costs and how the trip is put right by hand. **Shawn's to decide.**
- **Seen on the way, and not fixed:** "That change could not be saved" stands at the top of the Settings screen, above the first card, so for a press far down the screen it has the fault the red line had. It needs a settings file that cannot be written. Written down in the encyclopedia (Settings, gotchas) for a change of its own.
- **Tests:** 772 in all (766 before). New: which day a refusal stands under, each of the seven, and no day for any other problem (2 more than before); a phone set to 24 hours in a language with AM and PM, and the other way round; a day's hours on such a phone; the switch against eight languages; the language's own clock read from its hour letter (4 more than before).
- **Seen on an emulator afterwards** (Android 16, API 36, 1080 by 2400 px, `emulator-5730`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it through one script that refuses any other serial, shut down afterwards; the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read).
  - With Friday's two times just above the bottom bar: a start of 8:00 PM against an end of 4:30 PM closed the dial, left "Start: 8:00 AM", and moved the screen up 237 px, until the red line under Friday's times ended exactly at the edge. With the line scrolled out of view again, the same time picked once more moved the screen 221 px. A start of 7:00 AM was stored and took the line away.
  - With the phone's switch untouched (English): "Start: 8:00 AM", "End: 4:30 PM", a dial with AM and PM. Set to 24 hours while MilO was in the background, and MilO brought back without a restart: "Start: 08:00", "End: 16:30", and a dial that goes to 23 with no AM and PM. A trip recorded with the button and fixes from the console read "Started at 04:45" on Home, then "04:45 – 04:46" in Today and on Trips. Set back to AM and PM: "4:45 AM – 4:46 AM" on Trips, in the question before a delete and on Home. No crash.
  - **Not seen:** the phone turned with a red line showing (that the screen then stays put rests on the code); any of it on the phone, where HyperOS has its own date and time settings. Device checks 151 and 152.
- **Under `core/trip/`, `platform/bluetooth/` and `platform/trip/`: nothing.**

**`[CHANGE]` The work schedule and Business / Personal (the first piece of phase 2)**
Phone only. Run on an emulator (the finding below), never on the phone. Device checks 128 to 150.
- **The schedule** (Settings, a new card "Work schedule"): the seven days, each with a switch and, while it is on, a start and an end that open a time picker. Default Monday to Friday, 08:00 to 16:30. An end must be later than its start; a time that breaks that is refused in one sentence and nothing is stored. "Use these hours on every tracked day" under a day copies its hours to the others. A second card, "Trips outside the work hours": "Save as Personal" (the default) or "Ignore them".
- **The rule** (`core/schedule/`, pure): a trip is Business when its start, read in the phone's time zone, is on a tracked day at or after that day's start time and before its end time; otherwise Personal. A Business trip that ends after its day's end time is flagged "ran past schedule". What started the trip is not an input, so a trip started by hand follows the same rule.
- **Where it is applied: when a trip is finalised, and nowhere earlier.** The trip rules close a trip as before, and the write that closes it now also stores the category and the flag. **The schedule decides nothing about whether a trip starts or is recorded.** No file under `core/trip/` or `platform/bluetooth/` changed.
- **"Ignore them"** stops nothing from being recorded. A trip that turns out Personal is recorded whole and then stored as discarded and marked as ignored, with its distance, times, positions and GPS points. It is listed with the discarded trips, says why ("Left out: it started outside your work hours, and Settings says to ignore such trips."), and "Count this trip" puts it back as a Personal trip.
- **By hand:** a tapped trip on the Trips screen shows "Mark as Personal" or "Mark as Business" above "Delete trip". It writes the category and that Shawn set it, and nothing sorts that trip again. With "Ignore them" on, a kept trip marked Personal stays kept. One `TRIP` line per press, refused ones included.
- **Shown:** each trip's row says Business or Personal, and "ran past schedule" where it applies; each day's heading has the day's number of sessions and its Business km; the month card and Home's Today show Business first and large, and Personal in a line of its own, never added together. The Android Auto screen is unchanged and still adds every finished trip of today into one figure.
- **Storage: the main database is version 3.** Four columns on `trips`: `category` (text, empty means not sorted yet), and `categorySetByHand`, `ranPastSchedule`, `ignoredOutsideSchedule` (integers, 0). The step from 2 to 3 only adds them: no row is rewritten, and the migration sorts no trip. `app/schemas/…MiloDatabase/3.json` is exported; `1.json` and `2.json` are byte for byte what they were. No destructive fallback, as before. The settings file has 22 new keys: three for each day, and `ignore_trips_outside_schedule`.
- **Trips recorded before the update** are sorted at process start by a catch-up, in the pattern of the address catch-up, by the schedule as it is then. It takes only closed trips that have no category and were not set by hand, writes the category and the flag and nothing else, and never discards a trip.
- **Changed under `platform/trip/`,** the trip engine, all of it at the moment a trip closes, and nothing else there:
  - `TripLedger.kt`: `close` asks `fileTrip` what the schedule makes of the trip and hands the answer to `closeTrip`, in the same write as before; the ledger is given the phone's time zone as a function; `carryOut` is handed the settings reader in place of the one number it took.
  - `TripRuleSettings.kt`: reads the schedule and the ignore choice with the other settings (`filingRules`, empty while the settings cannot be read).
  - `TripLogText.kt`: the closing line ends with what the schedule made of the trip and what it went by, and says "discarded: it started outside the work schedule…" for an ignored trip.
  - `TripController.kt`: one more constructor argument, the time zone, passed on to the ledger.
  - `TripWorker.kt`: one line, the changed call to `carryOut`.
  - Not touched: `TripService`, the triggers, the start path, the preflight, how fixes are stored and counted, the notifications and the sound.
- **Design system, two deliberate additions:** `ChoiceRow` (one of a few named choices: a radio button with a second line) and `TimeDialog` (Android's clock dial in a dialog). `TimeDialog` wraps Material 3's time picker, which the pinned Material release (1.4.0) still marks as experimental; the opt-in is in that one file.
- **A new package, `core/schedule/`** (STANDARDS §3, ARCHITECTURE section 5). The trip rules' package was not to be touched, and the schedule is not a trip rule in ADR-002's sense: it takes no part in a trip, it sorts one afterwards.
- **No dependency and no permission was added.**
- **Tests:** 123 new, 766 in all: the rule at the edges of a day, on an untracked day, across midnight, on both days the clocks change and in another time zone (19); putting a closing trip away, with and without "ignore" (5); the schedule's own rules and the copy (13); which words a row uses (3); the schedule in the settings file (8); the totals with Business, Personal and unsorted trips mixed, and what a trip may be marked as (12); which trips the catch-up may touch, compared with the stand-in query (6), and what a pass does and says (11); the trip controller on stand-ins, sorted at the close and recorded in full whatever the schedule says (13); marking by hand (10); the month, a day's heading and a row (13); what the Settings screen shows (6); times and day names (3); the migration, now checked for every step (1).

**`[FINDING]` The migration to version 3 on an emulator, from version 2 and from version 1, and what has never run on the phone**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, `emulator-5712`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it through one script, shut down afterwards), with builds signed by the throwaway debug key. Three builds: version 1 from the commit before the addresses (`7cdb1de`, built from a copy of that commit's files), version 2 from the commit this work started on (`d02bfb3`), and this change.
1. **Real rows.** With the version 1 build: a 788 m trip (finished) and a 0 m trip (discarded), recorded with the Start and End buttons and GPS fixes fed from the console. The database was copied off: `user_version` 1, 13 columns, 29 log lines. Two more trips with version 1, then the version 2 build over it; with that, one trip deleted on the Trips screen and three more recorded. Copied off again: `user_version` 2, 17 columns, seven trips (five finished, one discarded, one deleted), 118 log lines, Room's identity hash that of `2.json`.
2. **Version 2 to 3.** This change's build was installed over it with `adb install -r` and MilO opened. No crash. The copy afterwards: `user_version` 3, `integrity_check` ok, 21 columns, the index, the identity hash of `3.json`. The 17 old columns of all seven trips and all 118 old log rows compared equal to the copy before, as text and by SHA-256. No status changed: the discarded trip is discarded, the deleted one deleted. `categorySetByHand` and `ignoredOutsideSchedule` are 0 on every row.
3. **The catch-up ran at that first start.** The emulator's clock was left alone, and its time zone was set to one in which the recording had straddled 16:30 on a Tuesday (Asia/Bangkok), so that the default schedule had something to decide. Six trips became Business, one of them "ran past schedule" (started 16:29:28, ended 16:30:32), and one Personal (started 16:30:40). One `TRIP` line: "Business or Personal set for 7 trips recorded before they were sorted (process start), by the work schedule as it is now: 6 Business (1 ran past schedule), 1 Personal. No trip was discarded or otherwise changed.", with each trip and its reason in the detail. The discarded and the deleted trip were sorted too.
4. **The chain from version 1.** The version 1 copy of step 1 was put back on the emulator (the three files, checked byte for byte after the write), with this change's build installed, and MilO opened. One start took it through both steps: `user_version` 3, `integrity_check` ok, 21 columns, the index, the identity hash of `3.json`; the 13 original columns of both trips and all 29 original log rows equal to the copy, by SHA-256. The finished trip then got its two addresses and both trips their category. **Both migrations were then run once more with the build that passed the gate,** on a fresh start of the emulator and from the same two copies: the same result, row for row and hash for hash.
5. **An older build over the new database fails, and loses nothing.** The version 2 build installed over the version 3 database died at its start with "A migration from 3 to 2 was required but not found". The database was untouched. This change's build installed again read every trip as before and wrote the crashes to the log. Android accepts the older build, because every build is version code 1. The rule is in ARCHITECTURE section 6 and in "Before the first check" of the device checklist.
6. **When the migration runs.** It runs the first time anything in MilO touches the database, which need not be Shawn opening it. Installed over a MilO that was running, the version 2 build had migrated the file before MilO was opened: Android tells an app that it was updated, and the boot-and-update receiver starts the process. Installed over a MilO that had just been force-stopped, this change's build did nothing until MilO was opened: Android sends a stopped app nothing. So the copy of check 128 is taken before the install, never after.
7. **The screens.** Trips: the month card "Business, 3.7 km, 4 trips" and "Personal: 0.9 km · 1 trip"; the day's heading "5 trips · 3.7 km business"; rows saying "Business", "Business · ran past schedule" and "Personal". Tapping the trip that ran past showed "Mark as Personal" above "Delete trip", moved into view; the press put the buttons away, the row said "Personal" and no longer "ran past schedule", and the totals became 2.5 km and "Personal: 2.1 km · 2 trips". Home's Today showed the same split. Settings: the two new cards; the dial opened as "Tuesday: end" on 04:30 PM, and 11:00 PM was stored and shown; "Use these hours on every tracked day" then stood under each of the five tracked days, and went away when Tuesday was switched off; a start of 11:00 PM against that end was refused with the red line, and nothing changed.
8. **Trips sorted as they end.** With Tuesday set to 08:00 to 23:00 a trip recorded at 16:35 was stored as Business, its log line ending "saved as Business: it started on a Tue at 16:35:48; that day's hours are 08:00 to 23:00 (Asia/Bangkok)". With Tuesday switched off and "Ignore them" chosen, the next trip started and recorded like any other (9 fixes, 865 m) and was then stored as discarded and ignored. The Trips screen counted one more trip as not shown, listed it on request with "Left out: it started outside your work hours…" and "Personal", and "Count this trip" made it a counted Personal trip, which then got its addresses. With "Ignore them" still on, a Business trip marked Personal stayed in the list. A restart after all that changed no row and wrote no second catch-up line.

**A defect seen on the way, and not fixed here:** when the older build died, two threads failed at the same moment, and the crash handler of one could not write its file: "Cannot create the crash folder". `CrashFileStore.write` looks for the folder and then creates it, and the other thread had created it in between. It can only happen while the folder does not exist yet, so for the first crash of an install, and the other thread's file was written. One line in `data/crash/CrashFileStore.kt`, left for a change of its own.

**What the emulator could not show, and what has never run on the phone:** all of it on HyperOS, and on the phone's own database, which holds Shawn's real trips; the time picker and the long schedule card under a thumb; a trip the truck starts outside the hours with "Ignore them" on; a real clock at 08:00 and at 16:30 (the emulator's edge was made with a time zone); a change of the clocks; anything with a screen reader. Device checks 128 to 150.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the schedule**
All are Shawn's to change.
- **A fourth column, `ignoredOutsideSchedule`.** The work order named three things to store: the category, whether it was set by hand, and the ran-past flag. "Marked discarded, with the reason" needs the reason where the Trips screen can read it, or a 12 km trip left out by the setting would say "Discarded: too short". A flag and not a new status: the status enum lives in `core/trip/`, which was not to change.
- **"Not sorted yet" is an empty `category`,** not a third constant. The migration then needs no default for it, and every reader has to say what it does with such a trip.
- **A change of the schedule sorts no stored trip again,** set by hand or not. The work order asked that a category set by hand survive a later schedule change; this goes further, because a month that has gone to accounts must not change when the hours do. The price: trips sorted by wrong hours are put right by hand, one at a time. A "sort this month again" button would be small to add, the rule is there.
- **The first catch-up after the update runs with the default hours,** Monday to Friday 08:00 to 16:30, before Shawn has seen the Settings screen. "Using the current schedule" was the order, and at that moment the default is the current one. The other way, waiting until he has looked at the schedule, leaves every earlier trip "not marked" for an unknown time.
- **The catch-up never discards a trip,** whatever "Ignore them" says: a trip that was kept before the update stays kept. And it sorts deleted and discarded trips too, so that Restore and Count this trip give back a trip that is already sorted.
- **Only a finished trip can be marked.** A discarded or a deleted one is counted or restored first. Storage refuses anything else, and the screen offers only what storage accepts.
- **A marking is made at once, and puts the buttons away.** It takes nothing away and the other button undoes it, so no question is asked. The buttons close because the same spot would at once hold the opposite button.
- **"Ran past schedule" is stored when the schedule sorts the trip, and a marking does not work it out again.** It is shown only while the trip is Business, so it disappears when the trip is marked Personal and comes back with Business. A trip that was Personal by the schedule and is marked Business never shows it.
- **A trip too short to keep is not also "ignored".** It is discarded for the older reason and says so.
- **With settings that cannot be read, a closing trip is left unsorted** and caught up later. The grace period and the minimum distance fall back on their defaults in that case; a category from hours that are not Shawn's would stay wrong for good.
- **The time zone is the phone's at the moment the trip is sorted,** which for a new trip is when it ends. (What that costs on a drive into another zone is in the review entry above. Left as it is.)
- **Hours are whole minutes, the end after the start, and the latest end is 23:59.** A refused time is refused whole: nothing is moved to make it fit. To move a day later, the end is changed first, and the message says so.
- **The copy button stands under each tracked day, only while it would change something, and asks no question.** Out of the box there is none on the screen. Days that are off are neither switched on nor given the hours.
- **A day that is off hides its two times and keeps them.**
- **Two radio rows, not a switch,** for "Save as Personal" and "Ignore them": the work order names both sides, and a switch names one.
- **The time picker is Material 3's dial.** The other way was two steppers per time; fourteen of those make a very long card, and the dial is what every Android phone uses.
- **The dial has AM and PM when the phone's language writes times that way,** like every time MilO prints, and not by the phone's own 24-hour switch. The two would otherwise disagree on the same screen. (**Reversed by the review entry above:** the phone's switch decides, for the dial and for every time printed.)
- **Personal is always on the month card and on Today, with zeros if need be;** the "not marked yet" line only while it has something to say.
- **A day's heading counts sessions of both kinds and adds up Business km only** ("3 trips · 42.1 km business"), as the brief's month view puts it: "sessions and business km per day".
- **Home's drive time is of the Business trips.**
- **The Android Auto screen's Today row was left alone,** as ordered, so it adds Business and Personal together. Whether the car should show Business only is open.
- **What the schedule made of a trip is on the trip's own closing line in the log,** with the day, the time and the hours it was judged by. A change of the schedule is not logged, like the other settings.
- **The catch-up lives in `data/trip/`:** it reads and writes storage and touches no system service.
- **The stored word is "category".** The Trips screen already has a `TripKind` (counted, in progress, discarded, deleted), and two kinds of kind in one file would be misread.
- **The gate was run with the throwaway debug key,** and the Keychain was not read.

**`[FIX]` The review of the rest of phase 1: nine findings, all of them real**
Two reviewers read the package below before it was committed and rated all nine findings minor. Each is listed with what was done. No table changed and no library was added.
- **An older build cannot read a deleted trip. Documents only.** The entry below says a new status value needs no migration. That holds going forward and no further. A reviewer deleted one trip with this build on an emulator and installed the build of the commit before (`30004b4`) over it: Android accepts it, because every build so far is version code 1, and its Trips screen then dies with `IllegalArgumentException: Can't convert value to enum, unknown value: DELETED` every time that month is opened. The trip service runs in the same process, so a trip being recorded is interrupted. No data is lost: this build installed again reads everything (`integrity_check` ok). **The rule: before any build from before 2026-10-06 is installed, every deleted trip is restored, or there is no going back.** It is in ARCHITECTURE section 6 (with the corrected convention: a constant can be added without a migration, and an older build crashes on it), in section 9, and in "Before the first check" of the device checklist. Not done on purpose: making Android refuse the older build by raising `versionCode`. The way past a refused downgrade is an uninstall, which deletes the trips (STANDARDS §13); a crash that the newer build cures is the smaller harm.
- **An audio file whose app answers with an exception ended MilO.** `OwnTripSound.choose` caught `IOException` and `SecurityException`. The picked file is opened by code of the app that holds it, and whatever that code throws arrives unchanged: an `IllegalArgumentException` or `IllegalStateException` from a file app that no longer knows the address it handed out. It escaped the Settings screen's coroutine and ended the process, the one the trip service records in. Every such exception is now a refusal like an unreadable file ("MilO could not copy that file. The sound was not changed."), with the exception in the `ERROR` line. Shown by the reviewer, and now by a unit test, with a stand-in for the picked file; never provoked with a real app.
- **The Delete button could come up below the edge of the screen.** It appears under the tapped trip, and for the lowest trip in view that was under the bottom bar: the tap looked as if it had done nothing. The row now asks the list to bring itself and its button into view (`BringIntoViewRequester`). Two details that are not obvious from the code. The request is made one frame after the button appears: the list answers from the row's size at that moment, and the button is laid out only in the frame after the tap (reasoned from Compose's own code; the version without the wait was not tried). And only a tap asks: a button that comes back because the list was scrolled, or the phone turned, leaves the list alone.
- **The minus button of a setting moved under the thumb.** The value between the two buttons was as wide as its text, so the minus button sat 14 dp further left at "1.5 min" than at "2 min", and every other press of a thumb held still missed. `StepperRow` now keeps a place of one width for the value, five times its text size. **A gotcha on the way:** the first version multiplied the text size by five and then converted to dp. At the phone's largest font size Android enlarges big text by less than small text, so the place came out narrower than "9.5 min" and the buttons moved again. The size is now converted first and multiplied after.
- **A deleted or discarded trip was a button that is switched off, and a short row was under 48 dp.** `TripRow` gave every row a click handler and disabled it for the rows that cannot be tapped; a screen reader calls that "disabled", right above a Restore that works. Only a finished trip has the handler now; the others are still read as one item. A finished trip's row is at least 48 dp high (Material's `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()`, so no dp value in the screen). It is set on the row itself and not on the row together with its button: there the spare 2 dp above the text would go whenever the button opened, and the text would shift.
- **Two lists of which change a trip may get.** `TripStatus.after()` and `correctionOffered()` were called by tests only, while the screen had its own table (`TripKind.correction`) and storage its own rule (the update matches on the status). `after()` is gone. `TripKind` now carries the stored status it stands for and takes its button from `correctionOffered()`, so there is one list. "Refused from every status but its own" is tested where it happens, through `TripCorrections` and `TripRepository` against the stand-in trips table. What no unit test reaches is the SQL itself (no Robolectric); it ran on the emulator in the entry below. `OwnSoundStore.copies()` existed for tests only and is removed; the tests list the folder themselves.
- **A separator written in Kotlin.** The "·" between a trip's times and its km in the delete question is now a string resource (`trips_delete_trip`).
- **The documents disagreed on what has run where.** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA still said the screens of work package 3 had "never been drawn anywhere", Setup had "never run on a phone or an emulator" and the sound was "never heard on the POCO X5", after "First results from the phone" (2026-10-05) and the emulator run below. Corrected there, in ARCHITECTURE (sections 8 and 10 had the same kind of sentence) and in the head of the device checklist, which now says: used once, no check run as written. Four entries edited by the package below still carried yesterday's date. The encyclopedia also said the sound card names Bedtime mode, and it did not; the card now does, since that is the mode that silenced the phone's first trip.
- **"Run before a phase is handed over" was still written in three places** (STANDARDS §11, ARCHITECTURE section 9, the head of the device checklist), against the decision at the bottom of this date. A session that reads STANDARDS first would have stopped before phase 2. All three now say what the decision says: the checks decide what counts as proven, and the next phase does not wait for them.
- **Tests:** 643 in all (641 before): three new (an exception from the other app, each change from each status through the repository, a row's kind against its trip's status), and `TripCorrectionTest` is one shorter without `after()`.
- **Seen on an emulator afterwards** (Android 16, API 36.1, `emulator-5566`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it, shut down afterwards; the throwaway debug key, the Keychain not read). Settings: the two buttons of both numbers at the same place for every value from "0.5 min" to "10 min" and from "0.1 km" to "2.0 km", at the usual font size and at the largest. Trips, with trips recorded there with the buttons: tapping a half-hidden trip at the bottom edge moved the list up 190 px, until the trip and "Delete trip" ended exactly at the edge; a trip in view whose button alone would have been cut moved it 19 px; the question read "2:18 AM – 2:19 AM · 0.6 km"; a deleted and a discarded trip were no longer reported as something to click; and a trip recorded in airplane mode ("Looking up the addresses…") had a row 126 px high, which is 48 dp there, whose text did not move when its button opened. **Not seen:** any of it on the phone, whose font is another one, and nothing with a screen reader. Device checks 125 to 127.
- **Under the three packages of the trip engine:** one `catch` in `platform/trip/OwnTripSound.kt` and a comment in `PickedAudio.kt`. Nothing else.

**`[CHANGE]` The rest of phase 1: delete and restore trips, today on Home, and a Settings screen**
Three things, phone only. Used on an emulator (the finding below), never on the phone.
- **Delete and restore** (Trips). Shawn asked "can we delete trips?" and was told how it would work; this is that. Tapping a finished trip shows "Delete trip", which asks first. A deleted trip leaves the list, the month's total, Home's Today and the Android Auto screen's Today at once, and is not destroyed: its row and its raw GPS points stay. The switch, now "Show deleted and discarded trips", lists it again, marked "Deleted by you. Not counted.", with "Restore". A discarded trip has "Count this trip", which makes it an ordinary finished trip. A trip that is still recording cannot be deleted. Each of the three writes one `TRIP` line to the event log.
- **Today on Home.** A card under the Start trip button: the total km, "N trips · (drive time) driving", and each finished trip of today with its times and km. The warning card and the current-trip card are as they were.
- **Settings** (`feature/settings/`), opened with a cog beside Home's title: the truck's name with "Change truck" (the pairing screen); the grace period, 0.5 to 10 minutes in half minutes; the minimum trip distance, 0.1 to 2.0 km in tenths; and the trip-start sound: on or off, Play, "Use my own sound" (Android's file picker; the file is copied into MilO's private storage and played from there) and "Use the built-in sound". A file that cannot be copied or played is refused in one plain sentence and the sound in use stays. Schedule, report and reminder settings are not there: they belong to later phases.
- **No table changed.** The main database is still version 2 and `app/schemas/` is byte for byte what it was. A deleted trip is a new value, `DELETED`, of the `status` column, which is text holding the enum's name: adding a value needs no migration (ARCHITECTURE section 6 says so, and the emulator showed it). Going back to an older build is another matter: the review entry above. The settings file has one new key, `custom_sound_name`.
- **Changed under the three packages the trip engine lives in,** and nothing else there:
  - `core/trip/TripStatus.kt`: the constant `DELETED`. The trip rules never produce it and never read it; no `when` over the status exists in the engine. It is there because the status column is typed by this enum, and the other way to mark a trip as deleted was a new column or table, which is a migration on the phone's real data.
  - `platform/trip/TripStartSound.kt`: a sound that is still playing is cut off by the next one (for the Play button; a trip start never meets it), and `playbackProblem`, the check that a file can be played.
  - `platform/trip/OwnTripSound.kt` and `PickedAudio.kt`, new: choosing a sound.
  - Nothing under `platform/bluetooth/`. `TripRuleSettings` and `TripService` are untouched: they already read the two numbers at every trigger and the sound at every trip start, which is what makes a change apply without a restart.
- **Design system, four deliberate additions:** `ScreenTitle` takes one icon button at its end (the way to Settings); `StepperRow` (a number between a minus and a plus); `ConfirmDialog` (the question before a delete; MilO's first dialog); `FigureRow` (text with a figure at its end, taken out of the Trips rows because Home's Today needed the same row). Three icons: a cog, a plus, a minus.
- **Shared, not copied:** `todayTrips` moved from `platform/car/` to `data/trip/TripTotals.kt`, as APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA had asked for the day Home needed it, with `isCounted`, the one rule for which trips count. The Trips screen, Home and the car screen all go by it. The car's hours-and-minutes arithmetic moved to `core/util/DurationFormat.kt` for the same reason. `HomeViewModel`'s stale "will show" comment about the Android Auto screen, left on purpose by the last package, is corrected.
- **No dependency was added,** and no permission: a pick in Android's file picker is itself the permission to read that one file.
- **Tests:** 87 new, 641 in all: which trips count and today's totals with deleted trips (8), the three changes as changes of status, a recording trip refused (7), the changes against the stand-in trips table with their log lines (12), the month with deleted trips (5), lengths of time (9), the ranges and steps of the two settings (10), the sound copies on a real folder (12), choosing a sound on stand-ins (10), what the Settings screen shows (9), and five in existing files (the back stack with Settings, the address rule for a deleted trip, the store's sound name, a deleted trip's address line; one test moved with `todayTrips`).

**`[FINDING]` The three things on an emulator, and what has never run on the phone**
An emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36.1, `emulator-5562`, read-only, without a window or sound, every adb call pinned to it and shut down afterwards), with a build signed by the throwaway debug key. The Keychain was not read: the gate too was run with `-Pmilo.signing.debugKey=true` and a keystore path that does not exist.
- **Trips.** Three trips recorded with the buttons and GPS fixes fed from the console: 1,181 m finished, 153 m discarded, and later 491 m. The finished one was deleted (the question, then Delete): the month went from "1.2 km, 1 trip" to "0.0 km, 0 trips" and "2 deleted or discarded trips are not shown." With the switch on it was listed as "Deleted by you. Not counted." with its two addresses; Restore gave back "1.2 km, 1 trip". "Count this trip" on the 153 m trip made it "1.3 km, 2 trips", and when the screen was read six seconds later that trip had its addresses. Cancel in the question changed nothing.
- **Home.** "No finished trips yet today.", then "1.2 km", "1 trip · 1 min driving" and the trip's line, with "The trip in progress is added when it ends." while the next one recorded; after the count "1.3 km, 2 trips", and after that trip was deleted again "1.2 km, 1 trip", without leaving the screen.
- **Storage afterwards,** from a copy of the files: `user_version` 2, `integrity_check` ok, statuses `FINISHED`, `DELETED`, `DISCARDED` as left, and every trip, the deleted one too, still with its fixes in `points.db` (12, 6 and 6). The four `TRIP` lines of the changes were in the log, word for word as the unit tests expect.
- **Settings.** The screen drew with its three cards. Plus on the minimum distance seven times gave "1.0 km"; plus and minus on the wait gave "2.5 min" and "2 min". **A trip recorded straight after, with no restart, was discarded with "491 m is under the minimum of 1000 m":** the change applied from the next event. "Change truck" (there: "Pair truck") opened the pairing screen and its arrow led back to Settings. With Settings open and MilO's process killed in the background, MilO opened on Settings again, with the changed numbers.
- **The sound.** Play wrote "Settings screen, Play pressed. Trip-start sound: playing the bundled chirp". "Use my own sound" opened Android's file picker. A text file named `.mp3` was refused: "That file cannot be played as a sound. The sound was not changed.", with `ERROR` "… refused (NOT_PLAYABLE)" and the player's own words ("Prepare failed.: status=0x1") in the detail. A real WAV file gave "Sound in use: your own sound, test-chirp.wav" and one file of the right size in `no_backup/trip_sound/`; Play and the next trip start both logged "playing the chosen sound" with no failure after. A second pick left one file, under a new name. "Use the built-in sound" left the folder empty.
- **A defect found there and fixed:** the truck card's button sat in the middle of the card. A card is only as wide inside as its widest line, and the button was aligned to that. It is now in a row that fills the width (APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Design system, gotchas).
- **What the emulator could not show.** Nothing was heard: it ran without sound, so "played" means the player accepted the file and reported no error. Two quick presses on a stepper could not be sent (two injected taps at once arrive as one); that both count rests on the code (one change at a time, each reading the stored value) and was not seen.
**Never run on the phone:** every screen and change above on HyperOS; HyperOS's own file picker and where it looks for audio; how a chosen sound comes out of the phone's speaker, and on vibrate; a grace period changed in Settings ending a real trip sooner (it needs the truck); the car's Today row following a delete. Device checks 101 to 124.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the rest of phase 1**
All are Shawn's to change.
- **Only a finished trip can be deleted, and only a deleted one restored.** A discarded trip is already out of every total, so it has "Count this trip" and no Delete. That keeps "Restore puts it back exactly as it was" true with the status alone: a deleted trip was always a finished one.
- **"Count this trip" is on every discarded trip, false starts included.** The row cannot tell "too short" from "a start that was never confirmed" for certain, and on a truck whose connection no reading can confirm (device check 32) a real drive would be discarded as a false start whatever its distance. Shawn decides by looking at the times and the km.
- **Delete asks first; Restore and Count this trip do not.** They take nothing away, and Delete undoes either. A trip counted by mistake therefore ends up "Deleted by you", not "Discarded" again: there is no way back to discarded, and none was asked for.
- **Tapping a finished trip shows the button; the other two kinds show theirs at once.** A list of counted trips with a Delete button on every row invites a slip. Deleted and discarded trips are only listed on request, and the button is why one looks at them.
- **A refused press is logged too,** as a `TRIP` line, so the log has exactly one line for every attempt, not only for every change.
- **The log line is written by the Trips feature** (`feature/trips/TripCorrections`), not by the trip controller: these changes never go through the trip rules, and routing them through the controller's inbox would have meant changing the engine.
- **A deleted trip keeps showing the addresses it had, and is not looked up while deleted.** A restored or counted trip is looked up at once.
- **Today's sessions are listed newest first,** like the days and the trips on the Trips screen, and **drive time is start to end of each finished trip,** stops inside a trip included (a trip is not split at a stop; kickoff decision).
- **Today sits under the Start trip button,** so the button does not move down the screen as the day fills up.
- **Home's "today" is worked out when Home comes to the front,** the rule the Trips screen uses for the current month. A Home left open over midnight shows the old day until it is opened again. The car screen checks the clock every 10 seconds; the phone screens were not given a timer for this.
- **Settings is opened from a cog in Home's title,** as the work order said (from Home), not from a fifth button in the bottom bar.
- **The ranges:** 0.5 to 10 minutes and 0.1 to 2.0 km. Under half a minute a truck that drops Bluetooth for a moment would end the trip; over ten, two drives start to run into one; no zero for the distance, or a start that never left the yard would count. Two constants in `data/settings/SettingChoices.kt`.
- **Plus and minus buttons, not a slider:** twenty exact values, set with a thumb in a truck.
- **Every press is stored at once.** No Save button, and a changed number is not written to the event log (each press would be a line); a trip's own lines show the values it ran with.
- **"A change applies from the next trip. A trip that is being recorded may already use it."** The work order asked for the first sentence. The second is there because it is true: the engine reads the values at every event, so a grace period that starts after the change, and the minimum distance at the end of the trip, are the new ones.
- **The chosen sound is copied into the no-backup folder,** 10 MB at most. In the backed-up files it could push MilO over Android's 25 MB backup limit, past which nothing at all is backed up. The price comes with phase 4: after a restore the settings name a copy that is not there, the chirp plays instead, and the sound has to be chosen again.
- **"Can be played" means Android's player can prepare the file.** Nothing checks its length or that it has a sound track.
- **A new copy is written beside the old one, and the old one is removed last.** With one fixed file name a choice that failed at the last step would already have overwritten the sound in use.
- **The name of the picked file is stored** (`custom_sound_name`), so the screen can say which sound is in use. It is a new key in the settings file, not a column.
- **Play plays even when the sound is switched off,** and through a player of its own, marked in the log as "Settings screen, Play pressed".
- **The gate was run with the throwaway debug key** (above), so that nothing in this work read the signing password. The signing set-up itself was not touched; a build for the phone is made the usual way.

**`[DECISION]` Phase 1 is finished and the later phases follow without a stop in between**
On 2026-10-05 Shawn said to finish phase 1 and then carry on through everything else in the brief without stopping between phases. That replaces the brief's "stop after each phase so Shawn can test on the phone", which APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA had carried since kickoff and now no longer says.
- **What changes:** the assistant does not wait for a hand-over between phases. Phase 2 starts when phase 1's work is merged.
- **What does not:** the order of the phases; the device checklist, which every phase still adds to; the rule that a phase's behaviour counts as proven only when its checks have run on the phone; and the stable-only, one-major-per-branch and documents-in-the-same-change rules.
- **What it costs, said plainly:** later phases are now built on trip detection that has never met the truck. If the first drives show that a trip does not start or end as designed, that is fixed first, and work built on top of it in the meantime may have to follow.
- The open points of "Sending to the accountant" (below, 2026-10-05) are not answered by this. They come up again at phase 3.

### 2026-10-05

**`[FIX]` The review of the addresses: three defects and four corrections to the documents**
Two reviewers read work package 4 and made nine findings, seven of them different. All were real. No fix touches the migration or the schema, so the emulator proof below stands as it is.
- **A trip closed by the restart rules had to wait for the next start to get its addresses.** `MiloApplication` asked for the address pass beside the reconcile, not after it. The pass read the trips while the stale trip was still open, and the lookup's watcher never saw that trip in progress (the controller closes it without ever publishing it), so nothing asked again. A reviewer reproduced it: closed as `FINISHED` (444 m, `STALE_AT_RESTART`), no `ADDRESS` line 15 seconds later. The request now goes through `TripController.whenCaughtUp`, so the pass runs once the reconcile is done. Proven by a unit test with a real controller and a truck reading that takes a moment; not run again on an emulator. Device check 87.
- **What is still not covered.** A trip the restart rules close later than at process start is not announced either: after the trip service could not be started (the trip is dropped from memory and closed by a later trigger), or when the truck is connected at the restart and the old trip is closed in the same step that starts a new one. The second is picked up by the pass at the new trip's first GPS fix, the first at the next occasion. Left alone: the cure is for the lookup to watch the stored trips, not the controller, and that is a larger change than this review.
- **A trip that started and ended offline got no line of its own.** The "no network" line is written once per set of waiting trips, and the set mixed the id of the trip in progress with the ids of finished trips. Trip N waiting as a start and trip N waiting as a finished trip looked the same, so "Waiting: 1 finished trip." never appeared and device check 85 would have failed on a phone that was doing the right thing. The two are now compared apart. Check 85 names both lines.
- **On Android 12 the 20-second limit did nothing.** There the geocoder call blocks its thread, and a time limit cannot stop a blocked thread: the lookup would have waited as long as the call took, then thrown a good answer away as late and counted an attempt. The call now runs through `runInterruptible`, which interrupts the thread at the limit. As far as is known Android 12's call answers an interrupt by giving up its wait and returning an empty list (its source is not on this Mac and was not read for this fix). Whatever it returns then is never used, because it comes after the limit. Proven only by a unit test of the wrapper on real threads: there is still no Android 12 device or emulator here, and the phone (Android 14) uses the other form.
- **A numbered and an unnumbered street address rank the same, and that is now what the documents say.** APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA ranked "house number, street and town" above "street and town"; the code never did. The code stays: the geocoder lists its best match first, and a numbered candidate further down the list can be the building next door or one on another street. A test pins it. Shawn's to change if the phone's geocoder turns out to put the bare street first.
- **`recordAddressLookup` is not safe to repeat,** and ARCHITECTURE section 6 and the class comment of `TripRepository` said every write to a trip is. Each call moves the time of the last attempt and can count one more. Both now say so; no code calls it twice.
- **Why not WorkManager** is now written into the decision below, and the pointer to the device check for a trip that ends by itself is 84, not 83.
- **Tests:** 5 new, 523 in all: the restart and the offline trip beside a real controller (2), the time limit around a blocking call (2), the house number (1).

**`[CHANGE]` From and to addresses on every trip (work package 4)**
The work order for this package quotes Shawn: "I just want to track from start address to end one for each trip." No map. Phone only. Addresses had been planned for phase 2 with the day view; this pulls them forward on their own.
- **Storage: the main database is now version 2,** with the first migration (`data/MiloMigrations.kt`). Four columns are added to `trips`: `startAddress` and `endAddress` (text, empty until looked up), `addressAttempts` (integer, 0) and `addressLastAttemptAtMs` (integer, empty). The same step adds the index on `startedAtMs` that the entry of this date further down left "for the first migration". No existing column, row or table is changed. `app/schemas/…MiloDatabase/2.json` is exported; `1.json` is byte for byte what it was.
- **Lookup** (`platform/address/`): Android's own `Geocoder` behind the small interface `AddressLookup`. The listener form from Android 13, the blocking form on a background thread on Android 12, an answer waited for 20 seconds at most. The answer is turned into one line by `addressLine`: house number and street, then the town ("10103 104 Avenue Northwest, Edmonton"); the street alone; the town alone; and last the geocoder's own line with the country and the postal code taken out. Never a country or a postal code.
- **When** (`TripAddresses`): when a trip stops being in progress, at process start, and each time the Trips screen comes to the front. Each occasion is one pass over every finished trip that is due, so trips recorded before this version are caught up the same way. No WorkManager and no new library.
- **Giving up:** a trip gets 4 lookups that leave an address missing, at least 2 minutes, 1 hour and 1 day apart; after the fourth it is not asked about again. With no network nothing is asked and nothing is counted.
- **The event log** has a new category, `ADDRESS`: one line per lookup of a trip with what happened to each end, one line for a trip in progress, and one line when a pass is put off for lack of a network (again only when what is waiting has changed).
- **The Trips screen** shows "from → to" under each finished trip's times, and under a trip in progress "From …" once its first GPS fix has an address. A missing address is said in words: "Looking up the addresses…", "no address found", "Where it started is not known yet." A discarded trip has no such line: it is never looked up.
- **A permission was added:** `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`, granted at install with no dialog (see the decisions below). MilO still has no internet permission.
- **Tests:** 54 new, 518 in all: the one-line address (12), the rule for what is due and when to give up (16), the screen's wording (10), the lookup as a whole on stand-ins (9), the migration against the two exported schema files (4), the month lines (3).
- **Under `platform/trip/`,** the reviewed trip engine: one change, `CurrentTrip` now carries the position of the trip's first usable fix, which the controller already held. Nothing that decides or stores a trip was touched; the lookup only reads the controller's published state. In the tests, the in-memory trips table moved out of `TripControllerFakes.kt` into `FakeTripDao.kt`, because two more queries took the file past 300 lines.

**`[FINDING]` The migration from version 1 was run on an emulator, and so was a failed one**
On an emulator started for this (Android 16, API 36, `emulator-5580`, read-only, every adb call pinned to it), with builds signed by the throwaway debug key, never the real one:
1. The build of the commit before this change (`75a316c`) was installed. Three trips were recorded with the Start and End buttons and GPS fixes fed from the console: 1,123 m finished, 0 m discarded, 999 m finished. 57 lines were in the event log. The database was copied off: `PRAGMA user_version` 1, 13 columns in `trips`.
2. This change's build was installed over it with `adb install -r`. MilO opened without a crash.
3. The database was copied off again. `user_version` 2, `integrity_check` ok, 17 columns, the index present, Room's identity hash equal to the one in `2.json`. The 13 old columns of all three trips, and all 57 old event-log rows, compared equal to the first copy, text and SHA-256 alike. The new columns were empty on the discarded trip; the two finished trips already held addresses, found by the pass at process start ("10103 104 Avenue Northwest, Edmonton → 9321 Jasper Avenue, Edmonton").
4. **A migration that fails loses nothing.** A throwaway copy of the project was built with one `ADD COLUMN` deleted from the step, and installed over a restored version 1 database. MilO died at start with "Migration didn't properly handle: trips". The database afterwards: still version 1, 13 columns, no index, every row equal to the first copy. The correct build installed over that one then migrated it, and wrote the earlier crash into the event log. Room runs a step, its check and the new version number in one transaction and rolls it back as a whole; this is that, seen.
5. On the same emulator: a trip in progress showed "Where it started is not known yet." and, ten seconds later, "From 10060 50 Street Northwest, Edmonton". With airplane mode on, End trip closed the trip at once, the row said "Looking up the addresses…", and the log had one "put off … no network connection" line for two passes. With the network back, opening Trips filled both addresses in.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the addresses**
All are Shawn's to change.
- **Four attempts, 2 minutes, 1 hour and 1 day apart.** "A small number, spaced apart" was the brief. The first wait is short because most failures are a bad moment; the last is long so that the final try is on another day. The numbers are constants in `platform/address/AddressRetry.kt`.
- **No network is not an attempt, and that cost a permission.** Without asking the phone whether it is online, a trip that ended somewhere without signal would spend its attempts on failures that say nothing about the place, and a day at a site with no coverage would give up on every trip of that day for good. Asking needs `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`. It is the check WorkManager's "connected" constraint would have made (the research recommended that route, B4), and WorkManager declares the same permission. ADR-002, amendment 26.
- **A failed lookup ends the pass.** If the geocoder gives an error (as opposed to "no address here"), the trips waiting behind are not asked about: they would fail the same way and each lose an attempt.
- **No scheduled retry, and why not WorkManager.** The research (B4) and ENGINEERING_STANDARDS section 2 both name WorkManager for this job, and it was left out all the same. This was not the builder's choice: the work order for this package put WorkManager out of scope and expected no new dependency. WorkManager is in the planned stack but not in the build yet (`gradle/libs.versions.toml` does not declare it), and adding it brings Room 2 onto the classpath beside Room 3 (ADR-001), which is a change for a branch of its own. It is not ruled out for later: it remains the planned tool when a retry that wakes MilO is wanted. Until then a trip whose lookup failed waits for the next occasion: the next trip ending, MilO being started, or the Trips screen being opened. Nothing wakes MilO for it.
- **The start of a trip in progress is kept in memory only.** A trip's row has no position until the trip closes, and this package was not to change how a trip is stored. The address is looked up from the controller's first usable fix, shown, and used again when the trip closes at that same position, so the geocoder is not asked twice. If the process dies in between it is simply looked up again.
- **The index on `startedAtMs` went into this migration,** as the entry below and ARCHITECTURE asked. It is not part of the addresses; it is here because every migration is a risk taken on the real phone, and this is the first one.
- **A new event category, `ADDRESS`,** in place of reusing `TRIP`: these lines arrive after a trip is over, sometimes days after, and would read as trip events.
- **The log lines name no address and no position.** The address is on the trip's row; the line says what happened.
- **A discarded trip shows no address line at all,** with the switch on. Its own note already says why it is not counted.
- **House number before the street,** as addresses are written in Canada. The geocoder's own formatted line is used only as the last fallback, because it carries the postal code and the country.
- **An address once stored is never replaced by a lookup.** There is no way yet to correct one by hand or to ask again for a trip MilO has given up on; both belong with trip editing (phase 2).
- **The home screen is unchanged.** The brief named the Trips screen.

**`[FINDING]` What the addresses have never been run on**
- **The phone.** Everything above was an emulator, where the geocoder is Google's and the network is the Mac's. On the POCO: whether `Geocoder.isPresent()` is true, what HyperOS's Play services returns for Shawn's yard and job sites (a rural site may give "Unnamed Road" or nothing), how long an answer takes, and whether the connectivity check reads true in the background on mobile data.
- **The migration on the phone's own database.** It ran on an emulator's version 1 database made by the same code. The phone's file holds trips and a log recorded on HyperOS. Device check 80 takes a copy off the phone before the update, so the worst case is a restore.
- **The moment after a trip ends by itself.** On the emulator trips were ended with the button, MilO in front. When the grace period ends a trip, the service stops and HyperOS may freeze or kill the process before the geocoder answers. Nothing is lost (the trip is caught up at the next occasion), but the address may then not appear until MilO is opened. Device check 84.
- **Android 12** (API 31 and 32), the blocking form of the call. Compiled, never run: the phone is API 34 and the emulator 36.
- **A lookup that fails or finds nothing,** against the real geocoder. Giving up, the waits and the wording for "no address found" are proven by unit tests only.

**`[DEBT]` A lookup that failed waits for an occasion, and a given-up trip stays without an address**
There is no scheduled retry: the work order for this package put WorkManager out of scope and expected no new dependency (more under the decisions above, "No scheduled retry"). A trip that ended offline gets its address when the next trip ends, when MilO is started, or when the Trips screen is opened, whichever comes first. A trip that used up its four attempts shows "no address found" for good; only a later change (editing a trip, or a "look up again" action) can fill it in. Cost to fix later: small, and it belongs with trip editing in phase 2.

**`[FINDING]` First results from the phone (POCO X5 Pro 5G, HyperOS 2.0)**
Shawn went through Setup on the phone the same evening, everything except pairing the truck, which waits until he is at the truck. Read back over adb afterwards, without changing anything:
- **All four runtime permissions are granted,** including location "Allow all the time", and MilO is on Android's battery-optimisation exemption list.
- **Background autostart reads "allow"** through the unofficial app-op (10008), and the same record shows it was rejecting nine minutes earlier. So the read works on HyperOS 2.0 and follows the switch.
- **A Start press before the permissions were granted was refused cleanly,** with the reasons in the log, and worked once they were granted. The first GPS fix arrived 0.3 seconds after the start.
- **The trip-start sound is silent in Bedtime mode.** Shawn heard nothing at first; the log showed the sound being played without error both times. His phone was in Bedtime mode, which silences notification-type sounds. With it off, his R2-D2 clip played. That is the behaviour he chose ("respects silent"), but it means a trip that starts while the phone is in Bedtime or Do Not Disturb gives no audible confirmation.
- **No crash and no error line.**
- Not yet known: whether each HyperOS button opened the right screen (he reported no problem), the companion consent dialog, and anything involving the truck.

**`[DEBT]` The Car App Library is pinned one release behind a security fix**
Found by the review of the Android Auto screen. `androidx.car.app` stays at 1.7.0, the newest stable release, by the stable-only rule. The release notes of 1.8.0-rc01 (2026-08-26) say "This release includes a security fix" and tell everyone on a lower version to update. They do not name what was fixed, and the commits behind it could not be read (`docs/research/2026-10-03-android-auto-screen.md`, finding 3).
- **Why it matters from this change on.** Until now MilO used only `CarConnection` from the library. The Android Auto screen adds `MiloCarAppService`, which is exported with no permission: any app installed on the phone can bind it. The host check that turns other apps away is the library's own code, so it is only as sound as the pinned library. The library's last named flaw, CVE-2024-10382, was of that kind: by its public record, an app installed on the same phone could attack an app through its `CarAppService`. That one is fixed in 1.7.0. Whether the unnamed fix concerns the service at all is not known. MilO's one process holds the trips and has location "all the time".
- **What was not done.** The release candidate was not taken. The rule is Shawn's, and so is an exception to it: the research puts the question to him ("Questions raised for the owner"). The other way out before 1.8.0 is stable is a build without the screen.
- **Cost to fix later.** One line in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` (`carApp`, tagged `TODO(debt)`), then the gate and device checks 88 to 100. **Take 1.8.0 the day it is stable.** Dependabot will offer it inside its weekly pull request of minor updates, where nothing marks it as more urgent than the rest.
- Read again on 2026-10-06 from the live release notes and the live Maven metadata: 1.7.0 is still the newest stable release, and 1.8.0 exists only as rc01.
Also written into ARCHITECTURE section 10 and APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA (Android Auto screen, gotchas).
**Closed 2026-10-06 (evening):** Shawn decided to take the release candidate as a one-time exception to the stable-only rule, and `carApp` is 1.8.0-rc01. The fix is in the build. What remains, the return to the stable line, has an entry of its own under that date ("The Car App Library is pinned to a release candidate"), and the tag in the version catalog now says that.

**`[FIX]` What the review of the Android Auto screen found in the device checklist**
Under "Before: Android Auto's developer mode", opening "Customize launcher" (check 89) came one step before "Install the build", and the build on the phone (below, 23:07) has no Android Auto screen. Followed in order, check 89 would have recorded "not listed" for a screen that was not installed, and Google's trusted-store rule would have been blamed for it. Installing is now step 1, Android Auto's settings are closed and opened again before the list is read, and checks 88 and 89 name the new steps. Both builds call themselves 0.1.0, so the checklist also gives an `adb` line that tells them apart. A change to the document only: neither the steps nor that line have been run.

**`[FINDING]` The Android Auto screen has never been run, and Google says it should not show on the truck**
The screen below was built without a phone, an emulator or a car: no adb, and Google's desktop emulator of a car display (the Desktop Head Unit) is not installed on the Mac and was not to be installed. What is proven: it compiles with warnings as errors, lint passes, and unit tests cover everything the screen decides (what each row says in every state, what counts as today, when it redraws, the wording of its log line). The pane itself cannot be built in a plain unit test, because the library's builders call into Android. Its make-up (three rows of a title and one line, one main button with a press handler, a header with a title and the app's icon) was instead checked against the rules compiled into the library, by reading them. That shows the library should not refuse it; it does not show what a car does with it.
**What has never run anywhere:**
- **Android Auto listing MilO at all.** Google's documentation says the "Unknown sources" developer setting "doesn't apply to apps built using the Android for Cars App Library", and that to test in a real vehicle "you must install it from a trusted source such as Google Play". MilO is installed from Android Studio, so by Google's words it should show in the desktop emulator and not on the truck. Other people's reports conflict. Shawn tests it on the truck; if MilO is not listed, the choices are a private Google Play install, a media-app style workaround, or dropping the screen (APP_ENCYCLOPEDIA, Android Auto screen).
- **The service, the session and the screen.** No car has bound the service, no template of MilO's has been handed to a host, and nobody has seen the pane, its three rows or its button.
- **The check of who may connect.** Whether the library's list of certificates lets this phone's Android Auto in.
- **Car API level 7.** Whether this phone's Android Auto speaks it. If not, MilO does not open.
- **The button,** and above all a trip started from the car with the phone locked: that start is made from the background.
- **The redraws:** that a redraw counts as a refresh (Android Auto's debug overlay shows the step count), and the 15-second gap.
- **The message for a refused start,** and every `ANDROID_AUTO` line of the screen in the event log.
- **HyperOS:** whether it lets Android Auto start MilO when MilO's process is dead.
All of it is in `docs/DEVICE_TEST_CHECKLIST.md`, checks 88 to 100.

**`[DECISION]` Points the documents did not settle, decided while building the Android Auto screen**
All are Shawn's to change.
